Opened for the facts the new words rely on: `docs/research/float-contract/issuance-miss-prereg-2026-10-06.md` (event definitions E1 and E2, the 424B3 and 424B2 exclusion, the blind spots), the SEC Form 8-K (sec.gov/files/form8-k.pdf, Item 3.02 text, read with pymupdf), and 17 CFR 230.424(b) (law.cornell.edu). Not opened: any built check; none exists yet, and Integration's ruling is quoted to me as "424B and 8-K item 3.02", with no form-type list.*

**Passes.**
- The structure: the lined-up clause names the check, the page names the two kinds of filing and what the check cannot see, nothing says "none", "audited" or "verified", and no new refusal string is added. 5p items 2 to 4 are met.
- The one line for him carries the cannot-see sentence and says a hit reads Cannot verify.
- Marketing's own reading that "Cannot verify: shares may have been issued since the filed count." covers a hit (a 424B5 can be a shelf set-up, which is capacity) is right.

**Required change 1: the words claim a wider check than the one that was measured, and that is the comfortable direction.**
"No prospectus or prospectus supplement filed since the count" reads as every prospectus-type filing. The only definition of the check that exists in the tree is the measurement's: E1 is a 424B1, 424B4 or 424B5 (a final prospectus for an offering or a shelf takedown) and E2 is an 8-K with item 3.02. **A 424B3 and a 424B2 are explicitly not events** (prereg, "424B3 (resale), 424B2, S-3 and S-1 filings alone are NOT events"). A 424B3 is a prospectus supplement in the filer's own words (the validation names carry "424B3 prospectus supplement"; Rule 424(b)(3) covers a prospectus "that reflects facts or events other than" the pricing cases, so it is the routine update, with a resale or a 10-Q attached, and it creates no shares). So if the built check follows the measured one, a name with a 424B3 filed since the count still prints a number under a popover that says no prospectus supplement was filed. That is a true-sounding sentence about a hazard stated wider than what the desk looks at. It is the same class as 5e to 5j (a bound whose condition is not the one the desk tests).
- Marketing's "for Commercial to confirm" (a 424B1 or 424B4 is a prospectus and a 424B5 a supplement): **not confirmed as a mapping.** Under Rule 424(b) the paragraphs are distinguished by what triggered the filing (price determination under Rule 430A, a Rule 415 takedown, other changes), not by prospectus versus supplement; "prospectus supplement" names a 424B3 as correctly as a 424B5.
- **The cure is to word the check from the built form list, not from the ruling's shorthand.** Either (a) the build refuses on every 424B, and "no prospectus or prospectus supplement filed since the count" is then true (an over-refusal is conservative, and the refusal string already says "may have been"); or (b) it refuses on 424B1, 424B4 and 424B5 only, and the clause must say what those are: "no final prospectus or pricing prospectus for an offering of shares" (not "any prospectus or supplement"). Research or Integration states which in one line; Marketing words from that line. Until it is stated the popover clause and the one line for him do not pass.

**Required change 2: the page's cannot-see list omits the largest blind spot the 8-K half has.**
Item 3.02 does not require a report for every unregistered sale. Form 8-K, Item 3.02: no report need be filed if the equity securities sold, in the aggregate since the last Item 3.02 report or last periodic report, are **less than 1 percent of the shares outstanding of the class, or less than 5 percent for a smaller reporting company**. Most names on this board are smaller reporting companies, so a private placement of up to a twentieth of the class files no 8-K at all. "No report of an unregistered sale filed since the count" is true of the check and still silent on that. The page's list ("sales under an open at-the-market program between supplements, and conversions or exercises that file nothing") should gain it: "private placements below the size that requires a report". The popover clause may stay as drafted (it says only what the desk looks for), and the page is where the limit belongs, as 5p item 2 asked.

**One precision, not a required change.** The page says "a prospectus or prospectus supplement, and a report of an unregistered sale of shares" are "two kinds made after the count". "Filed after the count" is what the check tests; a report is due four business days after the event, so a sale in the last days before the fetch date can be unreported yet. "Filings read on {fetch date}" already carries this and no edit is needed.

**What does not change.** 5p item 1: whether the 90-day maximum stands is his, with the 43-day BKYI case beside it. He ruled (2026-10-06, "keep 90, yes reset"; verified at his own turn: a human `enqueue` record in transcript d947056e at 2026-10-06T01:31:38.586Z, typed to Integration) that 90 stands; "yes reset" in the same line is Integration's seat reset. Nothing here reopens it. The note for him on the 90 days is Integration's to carry.

**Dependency.** Form B (the S1 present-tense wording) is separate and unchanged.


## 5r. The built form list, and the wording it licenses (5q required change 1 resolved), fourth tenure, 2026-10-06

*Read: `lib/research/floatJob.ts` lines 84 to 148 at `46b00886` (research/float-job), opened by me; Integration's relay of the same list was not relied on.*

**What the code does.** The check refuses when the submissions listing holds, filed on or after the count's as-of date (the count's own date inclusive) and up to the reading date D: a form that is exactly `424B1`, `424B4` or `424B5` (the code's own comment: "Final prospectuses for an offering or a shelf takedown. NOT 424B2, 424B3 (resale), S-1, S-3 or S-8"), or an `8-K` or `8-K/A` whose items include `3.02`. Every other form is not an event, including 424B2, 424B3, 424B7, 424B8, S-1, S-3 and S-8. The listing is the recent page only; if its earliest filing is later than the count date the job throws (`listingReaches`) and does not print a "no hit".

**Verdict on 5q required change 1: the build matches the measured list, so the cure is (b).** The clause "no prospectus or prospectus supplement" is false of this build (a 424B3 supplement and a 424B2 pass the check). The words must name what is checked, in terms true of that list, and must not use "prospectus supplement" as the description of the checked set. A wording this seat would pass: "no final prospectus for an offering of shares (SEC form 424B1, 424B4 or 424B5) and no report of an unregistered sale of shares (Form 8-K item 3.02) filed on or after the count date". "Filed since the count" is also acceptable and slightly understates the coverage (a filing on the count's own date refuses); the exact form is better.

**Does a resale 424B3 have to refuse for the claim to be safe? No.** A 424B3 registers resale of shares that already exist, so it adds none; a private placement that created them reports on item 3.02 if it is large enough, and below that size (required change 2) it files nothing, so refusing on 424B3 would not close that gap either. The surface only has to say what is checked and what is not. No design change is asked of Research.

**What the page may say about forms not checked.** Nothing is required. If Marketing wants to head off the "what about supplements" question, one true clause is: "resale prospectuses and routine prospectus supplements do not trigger it."

**Still owed on required change 2.** The page sentence on private placements below the item 3.02 reporting size (1 percent of the class, 5 percent for a smaller reporting company). The one line for him waits on both.


## 5s. Float draft v19 (`d5ca0adc`, the issuance check released), fourth tenure, 2026-10-06: PASS

*Read: `docs/marketing/float-page-draft.md` and `docs/marketing/float-wording-batch-2026-10-05.md` section 9b as the diff of `d5ca0adc` (local main, not on origin when read), plus every line of the draft that describes issuance (grep of the whole file). The build's form list is 5r, which I opened in the code; Marketing opened it too.*

**PASS.** The popover clause names the check as the code runs it: "no final prospectus for an offering of shares (SEC form 424B1, 424B4 or 424B5) and no report of an unregistered sale of shares (Form 8-K item 3.02) filed on or after the count date". The page names the same two kinds with the same forms, says resale prospectuses and routine prospectus supplements do not trigger the check (true of 424B2, 424B3, 424B7 and 424B8 in the code), adds the private-placement item with its 1 percent and 5 percent thresholds (Form 8-K item 3.02, read at the SEC form by me in 5q and by Marketing), and keeps the ATM and conversions sentence. Nothing says "none", "audited" or "verified". No other surface in the draft describes the check differently (the legend, aria-label, off-popover string and tooltip carry only "when the filings line up"; the Cannot verify strings say "may have been issued"). The one line for him in batch section 9b is true of the build and of the page. No em dash in either file (bytes, with a control).

**Two precisions, neither required.**
1. "Routine prospectus supplements do not trigger the check" sits one sentence after a 424B5 (a prospectus supplement in common usage) is named as a trigger. The words are true because of "routine", and the sentence before names the triggering forms, but a reader who stops at "supplements" could take a 424B5 as excluded; that is the safe direction (it understates what the check refuses on). If Marketing wants it exact: "Resale prospectuses and prospectus updates (such as forms 424B2 and 424B3) do not trigger the check."
2. "Item 3.02 requires none below 1 percent of the class" omits that the thresholds are measured in aggregate since the last item 3.02 report or periodic report, so a series of small sales can reach a report. Also the safe direction (the desk sees more than the sentence says), and the sentence is a cannot-see list, not a promise.

## 7c. Warning Shot and morning check words v2 (Marketing `842733f8`), fourth tenure, 2026-10-06: NOT READY, two required changes, one of my own passes withdrawn

*Read: `docs/marketing/warning-shot-confirmation-words-2026-10-06.md` at `842733f8`, whole; against the Warning Shot design at `26597e9d` and the Confirmation spec at `6840b616`, in particular the state definitions (design section 2 "States", section 6 "Reused dossiers stay NOT_YET"; spec sections 2 to 4). Not read: any built code. Both specs are on local main only.*

**Required change 1: drop "Main" from the label.** "Main risk in the evidence" asserts a ranking (the principal risk) that no guardrail can check (design section 4), which is the claim 7 item 4 asked the label not to make. The hover disclaims it ("not a ranking the desk checked"), but the label is what travels into reports and exports without the hover, and "main" carries what "largest" would have. Say "Risk in the evidence" (or "A risk in the evidence") and, in the hover, "its reading of a risk the evidence shows for this name". **This is partly my own doing:** 7 item 4 itself said "the desk's reading of the main risk in the evidence" next to "does not claim the risk is the largest"; the constraint contradicted itself and Marketing built to its first half.

**Required change 2: the Unknown hover is false for one of its two causes.** "The desk could not establish a risk from its research for this name." The state UNKNOWN is "the model returned null, **or a guardrail removed the text**" (design section 2). A removed sentence means the desk did establish a risk and withheld its wording; "could not establish" is untrue of that name, and the face "Unknown" reads the same way. The ruling I wrote in 7 item 4 ("must say the desk could not establish a risk") was too narrow for a state I had read the definition of. Say: "The desk is not showing a risk for this name. That is not a finding that there is no risk." True of both causes, and it still never reads as "none".

**Passes, and one pass of mine withdrawn.**
- **"Not recorded" for NOT_YET: passed, and the spec's "not researched yet" is withdrawn.** NOT_YET is "the dossier predates schema version 3", and design section 6 recommends that reused dossiers stay NOT_YET, so a researched name carries it for as long as its evidence is unchanged. "Not researched yet" is false of those names and promises something is coming, as Marketing says. 7 passed the spec's string after reading sections 1 to 4 of the design; the case that breaks it was in section 6, which I had not opened. The hover "This name's research was written before the desk recorded this field." is true of both a reused and an old dossier.
- The Unknown face "Unknown", NO_EVENING_CALL printing nothing, UNKNOWN on the morning check printing "Unknown" with no reason, and the four state names never printing: pass.
- "Morning check" as the label and its hover (names the four parts compared and says nothing else is): pass. "Confirmed" appears in no printed string.
- CONFIRMED: "Catalyst and setup grade unchanged since {evening ET}" with "Compared: {evening ET} and {morning ET}" always under it: pass (it names what was compared and dates both ends). Its detail says "The desk re-read this name's research after the evening checkpoint"; the spec counts a re-check as a new version or a COMPLETE job in the window, and a job whose evidence is unchanged reuses the interpretation. "Re-read" is fair for the job having run and the interpretation standing; no change required.
- NOT_RECHECKED "Not re-read since {evening ET}" with "The setup grade is the same" (true by the spec's order: a grade change reads REVISED first), NOT_YET "No morning check yet" with "No morning checkpoint has completed for this date", REVISED with from and to and "not stated" for a null, and the rule that REVISED never says the research was re-read: pass.
- Design's two points in section 3 (the grade through the Setup mapping so AVOID and "Priority" do not leak; catalyst enums through the board's display words): correct, and they are claims points as well as build points.

**Recommended, not required.** "Compares the evening call (the {evening ET} recap)" uses "call", which on a named stock reads as a trade call. "The evening reading" or just "the {evening ET} recap" says it without the advice register.

**Still owed to this seat before any real-name run** (unchanged from 7b): the built code's literal prompt string and word list, read against the design text.


## 8. Two small drawer hovers (Marketing's `drawer-rows-words-2026-10-05.md` and `drawer-rows-18-13-24-words-2026-10-06.md` at `0e1e90e7`), fourth tenure, 2026-10-06

**The country hover: not passed; true of one source and the build reads another.** The hover is "Country is where the company's business address is, from its SEC filings." That is exact for EDGAR's `addresses.business.stateOrCountry` (`lib/research/sec/submissions.ts:131`), decoded by `lib/research/sec/stateCountry.ts` (US state codes read as the United States, and "CA" is California, not Canada). But the field contract maps row 4 to **dossier `country`** (`docs/research/sheet-reproduction/proposal-2026-10-04.md:94`, final in `field-contract-final-2026-10-05.md:61`), and `stateCountry.ts` says in its own comment that `dossier.country` "is MODEL-WRITTEN and mixed": on 2,500 prod versions mostly EDGAR codes copied from the bundle, but also names the model wrote ("Cayman Islands" 67, "Israel" 27, "United States" 16, "US" 15), and `countryName` maps a name to a country without checking it against EDGAR (not re-measured by me; the counts are the file's). So for roughly one dossier in twenty the printed country is the model's word, and "from its SEC filings" is not established for it. Two ways to make it true, and which is a build choice for Design and Research: (a) the drawer's country is `edgarCountry` of the registry code (`issuer_registry.country`), which is the SEC business address by construction, and the hover stands as written; or (b) it stays `dossier.country` and the hover says what is true of it ("Country as recorded in the desk's research"), with the SEC claim dropped. Until one is stated the hover does not pass. The hover is otherwise well made: it says business address, not origin or incorporation.

**The empty supply-group hover: pass, conditional on one fact I did not open.** "The desk's research holds nothing here. Either it found none or it did not read for this group; the record does not say which." with the face "Nothing recorded for this group." is true of both causes and never reads as "none exist". It rests on a negative about the record ("does not say which"). The interpretation bundle carries a deterministic `coverage` summary (`lib/research/interpret/bundle.ts:150`); I did not open whether any per-group "searched, found none" marker reaches the stored dossier the drawer reads. If one does, the hover understates it for those groups and Design should show it; if none does, the sentence stands. Marketing or Design state which in one line.

## 7e. The built Warning Shot strings (Research `1822d376`, branch research/warning-shot), fourth tenure, 2026-10-06: PASS on the strings with one required change before a real-name run

*Read: `lib/research/interpret/evidenceRisk.ts` whole, the diffs to `schema.ts`, `openaiInterpreter.ts`, `pipeline.ts` and `guardrails.ts`, the build note, and the names of the tests, at `1822d376` (not on origin when read). Compared with the design at `26597e9d` by script (it extracted the 8 rule parts from the code and the rule paragraph from the design and compared the two strings). Not read: the test bodies beyond the em dash and May cases, and nothing has run: the build note says no tsc, eslint or vitest has run, so this pass covers the strings and lists, not that the build works.*

**Verbatim and complete.**
- `EVIDENCE_RISK_RULE` equals the design's section 3 rule byte for byte (1121 characters, 8 parts joined by one space); the good and bad examples are the design's. The rule is in both the prompt (`evidenceRiskPromptBlock`) and the schema description, under the neutral key `evidenceRisk`.
- `EVIDENCE_RISK_FORBIDDEN` carries every group from 7 item 2: the report checker's shapes (copied, and each one compared by eye against `lib/desk/reports/check.ts` `FORBIDDEN` at origin; the omissions are the report-only shapes "quiet", fit as a forecast and a count of names, as the code comment says) plus "avoid", all eighteen modals and likelihood words, all nine exposure and danger words, all seven characterisation words, the engine-constant shape and the em dash with its entity forms. `EVIDENCE_RISK_ACTIVITY` carries the 7a words (issued, issuing, issuance, sold, selling, sells, dilute, dilutes, diluted, diluting, dilutive, dilution, raised, converted, exercised; "being sold" is covered by "sold" and "selling"). The 40-word cap and the one-sentence rule are in. The em dash pattern in the source holds its escape (`\u2014`), and the test builds the glyph with `String.fromCharCode(0x2014)` and expects a sentence containing it to be removed, so the check can see a planted case once the tests run.
- Guardrail 4 reads the corrected ledger; guardrail 5 carries `float` beside a number and a dated share count.
- States: UNKNOWN covers null and a removal, never the removed text; NOT_YET is schema below 3. This matches the Unknown hover (v3) and the Not recorded string.

**The narrowing is accepted: "May" before a number is the month.** `\bmay\b(?!\s+\d)` removes the modal in every grammatical use (a modal is followed by a verb, never a digit) and keeps "effective on May 5, 2026" and "filed in May 2026". It only narrows a removal and errs safe elsewhere ("In May, ..." is still removed).

**Required change before a real-name run: the good example carries a concrete figure and a claim of absence the model can copy.** "The supply ledger shows an open at-the-market program of $25 million, with no sales recorded." is in the prompt for every name. No guardrail checks a dollar amount (guardrail 5 checks share counts and "float" only), so if the model reuses "$25 million" on a name whose program is a different size, or "with no sales recorded" on a name whose ledger is KNOWN_ACTIVE ("sales" is not an activity word), the sentence passes every rule and states something false about a named company. This is a claim about how the model uses an example, not measured here; the cure costs one string. Use a placeholder and drop the tail: "The supply ledger shows an open at-the-market program of {amount}." (still passes every rule: no modal, no activity word, no number). The bad example stays. **This is my own: the tail was in my 7 item 1 example and I passed the design's figure in 7a, checking the example against the rules and never against being copied.** The test that the good example survives (V1) is unaffected by the change.

**Precision, not required.** The lists do not carry the noun "risk" or the near words "threat", "threats", "warning", "warns", "concern". "At risk of", "faces" and "risks" are removed; "a delisting risk exists" passes and is a condition stated, which is what the field is for. The removal audit at ship will show whether any of these appears; if one does, add it then.

**For the surface build (Design), a requirement that the code cannot show yet.** The stored `evidenceRisk.removed.text` is a sentence a guardrail removed, which by construction can be a forecast or characterisation of a named company. It must never reach a browser payload, an MCP tool result, a report, an export or a log a user reads: every surface takes `evidenceRiskState` only (it returns the rule that removed it, never the text), and a test pins that a payload built from a dossier with a removal carries no part of `removed.text`. Nothing reads the field today but the validator; the payload does not exist yet, so this is a condition on it.

**Release.** With the example changed, the strings and word lists are released from this seat for the first real-name run, in an Ops slot, with the ship conditions in the build note (his yes by literal sha, the at-ship cost measure). The Warning Shot's sentence still needs "as of {checkpoint time} ET" on every surface (7a item 2) and the cited sources beside it or no "cited" claim (7 item 5): Design's.

**Recorded here, 2026-10-06: his ruling on the country hover.** "yes, ship it" at 2026-10-06T01:43:44.168Z (human `enqueue` record in transcript c9a1acbc, Integration's chat), to a question that carried this seat's hold and its acceptable forms accurately. Cut 3 ships with whichever country hover is in it; the SEC-sourced sentence is then live and wrong for the dossier-written and Sheet-fallback cases until Design's fix (`e105d9e6` carries it) is released. The hold was a recommendation and his ruling stands; nothing further is owed from this seat on the string except the read of the built string when the fix is built.

## 7g. What "Risk in the evidence" is dated to, fourth tenure, 2026-10-06 (Design's question; corrects 7a item 2)

*Read: `lib/research/interpret/pipeline.ts` lines 180 (the bundle's sources read) and 383 (`generatedAt` set after the model call), `lib/research/types.ts:606` (`generatedAt` on the dossier), and Design's account of `readModel.ts:996-1014` (the live drawer reads the current dossier version, not a checkpoint's).*

**The sentence is "as of the bundle's date", and the bundle is read at interpretation time.** So the instant the sentence is true of is the dossier version's own time, `generatedAt` (set a minute or less after the bundle was read; at the minute, the two are the same). It is not a checkpoint's `completed_at`. A checkpoint row cites a version that can have been written days before the checkpoint (a version stays current while its evidence is unchanged), so printing the checkpoint time beside the sentence dates it to an instant its evidence may not reach. **7a item 2 asked for "as of {checkpoint time} ET" by analogy with the grade tooltip; that was wrong for this field**: the grade is computed at the checkpoint, the sentence is not.

**Ruling.** On every surface (live drawer, MCP `get_ticker`, frozen board, reports, exports) the sentence carries "as of {the dossier version's generatedAt, ET, to the minute}" with the date when it is not the reader's day. On a checkpoint surface the version is the one the row cites. A sentence whose version has no `generatedAt` is not shown (an undated sentence is not printed, as with the float's count date). The checkpoint time may appear elsewhere on the row for the grade; it does not date this sentence.

**Ledger.** Entry 5 of the fourth-tenure ledger in the charter.


## 9. Drawer row 5 words (Marketing `7bfc267d`, `drawer-row-5-words-2026-10-06.md`): close, after hours, premarket, fourth tenure, 2026-10-06: NOT READY, three required changes

*Read: the file whole at `7bfc267d` (local main, not on origin when read); against `lib/market/engine.ts` (the rth close and after-hours coverage code: lines 452-466, 812-860, 880-990, 1070-1110), `lib/market/types.ts:55-93`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:59`, and `lib/desk/view.ts:134`. Alpaca's own documentation was fetched on two pages (WebFetch's summary, not the pages read in full): it does not say whether daily bars include extended hours or whether the daily close is the official close. Nothing in the premarket strings is built, and I did not read them.*

**Required change 1: the "16:00 ET" clock is false on an early-close day.** The engine takes the regular-session close as an input (`rthCloseMinutes`, "Defaults to 16:00; an early-close day must pass the calendar's real close"), the schedule takes `closeMinutes`, and the session description has an `earlyClose` flag, so the desk models days when the bell is earlier. Strings that say "16:00 ET" are then wrong: the after-hours face "Today's after hours begins after the 16:00 ET close", the C3 hover "as of the 16:00 ET bell", the volume hover "after the 16:00 ET bell", the prior-session hover. Say it without the clock (or print the day's real close time from the same calendar value Design already holds): "Today's after hours begins after the regular-session close", "as of the bell on {date}", "Counts only trades after the bell".

**Required change 2: the close is called a regular-session close where its source does not say so.** `RthCloseSource` has four values: the two daily-bar sources, `simulator` and `sheet`. C5 ("The regular-session close for {date}, as stored. The desk does not show its source.") asserts "regular-session" for a value whose source is the Sheet or unknown, and the percent hover ("Measured from the regular-session close shown above") repeats it for every source. Use: sheet source, "The close for {date} as the Sheet recorded it."; unknown source with a value, "A close for {date}. The desk did not record where it came from."; and the percent hover "Measured from the close shown above, for {date}." A `simulator` value must not reach a user surface at all.

**Required change 3: the "not recording" hover (A5) does not match what the engine's NONE means.** In the engine `ahCoverage` is NONE only while the regular session has not closed (it is the reset state at `setRthClosed(false)` and the initial value before the bell); after the bell it is COMPLETE (the desk watched the bell, or a seed filled the window) or PARTIAL (the desk started after the bell and has not been seeded). So "The desk was not recording after hours on {date}" is not the meaning of any state the engine produces after the bell. Design states in one line which payload state A5 is for (a stored row of a day the desk was not running, a missing row, something else) and Marketing words it from that; before the bell, NONE must read as the schedule fact (A1), never as a recording gap.

**Recommended, not required.** (a) **A4's cause.** "The desk began recording after the bell on {date}" names one cause. PARTIAL is "observed live: false" and unseeded, which is a process that began after the bell; if the stored row can reach PARTIAL by any other route, a string true of every cause is safer: "The desk did not see every trade after the bell on {date}, so a volume would leave out some." (b) **C3** rests on the engine's own comment that the frozen close "starts as the last regular-session trade the desk saw"; the freeze takes `todayRthClose.close ?? runtime.price` when the session is told it has ended, so Design confirms that the price at that moment is the last trade before the bell and not one after it.

**Passes.** The group label "Sessions" and the line labels; C1 and C2 "The regular-session close from the daily bar for {date}." (see below); C3 apart from the clock, with "The daily bar's close can differ"; C4 "Unknown" with "The desk has no regular-session close recorded for {date}."; A3 "No close" with "The desk has no regular-session close to measure the change from." and no "yet"; no "official" and no "closing auction" anywhere; no forward tense.

**Marketing's question (official close or last trade?).** Keep "official" and "closing auction" out. The repo's own check says the daily bar's close was "verified against minute bars to be the official closing-auction print" (`types.ts:70-75`, `ARCHITECTURE.md:59`), but that is an internal verification (the number of names and the date are not stated) and Alpaca's public pages do not say it. "From the daily bar" is attributive and true; it credits no definition to Alpaca, and that is why it passes.


## 9b. Drawer row 5, additions A6 and PARTIAL-with-no-trade (Marketing `f35d945f`), fourth tenure, 2026-10-06

*Read: the diff of `f35d945f` against `7bfc267d` (local main, not on origin when read). The file still carries the three 9 required changes (the "16:00 ET" clock, the "regular-session" close on a Sheet or unknown source, the A5 "not recording" hover); Marketing's message predates 9 and none of those is re-read here.*

**A6 (after the bell, complete coverage, no after-hours trade seen): two required changes.**
1. **The clock** ("No trade seen since the 16:00 ET bell"; "after the 16:00 ET bell on {date}"): 9 required change 1 applies. "No trade seen since the bell"; "... saw no trade after the bell on {date}."
2. **"The desk was recording" is not what COMPLETE means.** In the engine COMPLETE is "observed live from the close" or "seeded" (`seedAfterHours` fills the window a restarted desk missed, and sets COMPLETE), so on a seeded name the desk was not recording the whole time. A string true of both: "The desk's after-hours record for {date} is complete and holds no trade after the bell." The face "No trade seen since the bell" is fine: "seen" keeps the claim about the record, not the market, which is the right instinct.

**One thing the words depend on and I cannot read from here:** what "no trade seen" is computed from. It must be "no trade with a timestamp at or after the bell in the record", not an empty price, a price equal to the close, or a zero volume. Design confirms which, because the wrong test prints "no trade seen" on a name that traded and whose price came back to the close.

**PARTIAL with no trade seen (price and percent "Unknown"): pass in meaning, with the clock-free form and the cause caveat from 9.** "The desk began recording after the bell on {date}, so it cannot say whether trades occurred before it started." says what it cannot say, which is the claim; it names one cause of PARTIAL (a process that started after the bell), and 9 recommended (a) the cause-neutral form ("The desk did not see every trade after the bell on {date}, so it cannot say whether trades occurred.") if any other route can reach PARTIAL.

**Design's seven placeholders mapped to the words:** the mapping is consistent with 9 and moves nothing, except that `closeOther` still maps to the C5 hover 9 required change 2 rewrites, and `afterHoursNotRecorded` to the A5 hover 9 required change 3 holds.

## 9d. Drawer row 5: Design's code answers (cut 4), fourth tenure, 2026-10-06

*Read: Design's account of `engine.ts` 799, 945, 950, 1213, 1617-1619, 1629, `worker/runtime.ts:1688-1689`, `config.ts:142`, `types.ts:561` and `readModel.ts:663`. I read `engine.ts` 799-990 and the freeze code myself in 9; the later lines are Design's reading, which I did not open.*

**(a) The clock.** Strings are clock-free after 9 and may stay so. If Design adds the day's real close time, it comes only from `MarketSnapshot.discovery.rthClose`; where `readModel.ts:663` falls back to the constant '16:00' (session summary missing) the string carries no time. Design's handling is right.

**(b) A5 is unreachable live.** Accepted: the NONE-after-the-bell branch is a defensive "Unknown" with no hover. Marketing's A5 and the prior-session NONE string stay unbuilt until a source can produce the state.

**(c) C3: the string is not true as worded, and a required change follows.** The freeze runs in the flush that first sees the clock past the close (about one second), and `runtime.price` takes any newer print regardless of its time, so the frozen price can be a print up to about a second AFTER the bell. "The last regular-session trade the desk saw, as of the bell" claims a trade at or before the bell. Say what is true: "The last trade the desk saw around the bell on {date}. The daily bar's close can differ." (No "regular-session": a print just after the bell may not be one. The line label "Regular session close" stays; the hover says this value is provisional and can differ, which is what carries it until the daily bar arrives.)

**A6: the test is right, and the hover must claim only what the test establishes.** "No trade seen" is `afterHours.high === null`, where `ahHigh` is set only by a trade, minute bar or seed timestamped at or after the bell. That is the right test (not price, not volume, not equality with the close). Its known edge: the freeze resets `ahHigh`, so a print in the up-to-one-second gap between the bell and the freeze is absent until that minute's bar re-adds it, so for up to about a minute a name that printed only in that second can read as having none. The strings must therefore claim the record and not completeness or the market: **hover "The desk's after-hours figures for {date} include no trade."** (replacing both "was recording and saw no trade" and 9b's "record is complete and holds no trade", since "complete" is also untrue in that window); the face "No trade seen since the bell" is acceptable, and "No after-hours trade recorded" is exact if Marketing prefers. The edge is recorded here as a known limitation and needs no wording on a face.

**(d) Simulator.** Agreed: a `simulator` close prints no close (Unknown, no hover); C5 splits Sheet and unknown source.

## 10. The one-day run wording (Marketing `3409b2c2`, asked by Integration 29th), fourth tenure, 2026-10-06: not as worded, one required change

*Read: the diff of `3409b2c2` (local main, not on origin when read) and `lib/market/cycle.ts` at origin (`isCycleStart`, `resolveCycle`, the prior-cycles loop, the `CycleArc` fields).*

**What the computed cycle's one-day run is.** A run's start is the most recent session that closed at least 20 percent above the prior session's close on at least 5 times normal volume (`isCycleStart`: `closeChangePct` and `dailyRvol`). Its high is the highest intraday high at or after the start in the settled daily bars (`cycleHigh`, `cycleHighDate`), bounded for a prior run by the next start; the advance is from the prior session's close (`baseClose`) to that high; "sessions ago" counts from the high to the latest settled bar, and the bar set never contains the live session. A "one-day run" is a run whose start date equals its high date.

**"Began and peaked that day" is true of the stored dates and is not safe as a statement about the move.**
1. **"Began" is the desk's start rule, not when the move began.** The session qualifies by its own close-to-close change and volume. The stock can have been rising the session before without qualifying (the base close is then already higher), so "began that day" claims a fact about the market the cycle does not establish.
2. **"Peaked" is the highest high so far in the settled bars, and for a current run it can be stale.** The high is the latest settled session when the run is a day old (phase DAY_ONE, "the latest session"), and the live session is not in the data, so a name that started yesterday and is higher this morning still reads "peaked" yesterday. For a prior run, "peaked" is within its own bounded window and is fine.

**Required change: state the two stored dates, as Marketing's own note says the words do, and keep the market fact out.** "Current run: {date}, start and high on the same day: up {X}% from the base close to the high, high was {N} sessions ago" (and "{date}, start and high on the same day: ..." in Prior runs). The "N sessions ago" tail and its rules ("1 session", "the latest session") stand; the range form for differing dates stands. No wording of the form "began", "peaked", "ran and faded" or "one-day wonder" for this case.


**10a. Pass recorded (Marketing `e0f9192b`), 2026-10-06: PASS.** Current run "Current run: {date}, start and high on the same day: up {X}% from the base close to the high, high was {N} sessions ago" and Prior runs "{date}, start and high on the same day: ..." state the two stored dates and no market fact; the tail rules and the range form for differing dates are unchanged; the old phrase appears nowhere in the file; no em dash (bytes and entity form, 0). Design had built the superseded text on its own tag and replaces it before shipping.


**10b. Built string passed (Design `a1d3b7fa`, tag design/one-day-run-a1d3b7fa), 2026-10-06: PASS.** `CYCLE_WORDS.oneDayRun` is "start and high on the same day" and `runLine` prints "{date}, start and high on the same day: up {X}% from the base close to the high, {N} sessions ago", with "Current run: " in front for the current run; a run whose dates differ keeps "{start} to {high}". The text "began and peaked" appears nowhere in the tag (the earlier commit `d1c54b5f` is superseded and not for shipping). The runs hover ("a day the stock rose at least 20% on at least 5 times its usual volume") is unchanged and not part of this read.


## 11. Row 21, the morning catalyst words (Marketing `90083552`, `docs/marketing/pm-catalyst-words-2026-10-06.md`), fourth tenure, 2026-10-06: NOT READY, three required changes

*Read: the file whole at `90083552` (local main, not on origin when read) against the Confirmation spec section 7 (at `6840b616`: the states, "newer" defined as two different version ids with the morning version's `generated_at` later, `catalystChanged` as any difference in the compared catalyst parts, the clock only when a cited exact source carries it). Not read: `pmCatalystOf` (Marketing did not read it either).*

**Required change 1: "{date}, time not stated" is a claim the payload can falsify.** The spec's payload carries `eventClock`, set when a cited exact source states a time. Printing "time not stated" for an event whose source does state one is false, and "this build carries no clock" is a display choice, not a fact about the event. Print the date alone ("{date}"), and when the event date is absent say so ("No event date"), never a statement about whether a time was stated. If Design later shows the clock it does so under the reports' `eventWhen` rule.

**Required change 2: "A later research version changed other parts of the dossier" is not established.** `NEWER` with `catalystChanged` false means a different, later version exists and that grade, status and event date match. Nothing compares the rest of the dossier, and a version can be written with only reworded text. Say what is known: "Catalyst grade, status and event date are the same as at the {evening ET} recap. The research was written again after that recap." (the Written line then dates it). The first sentence stands: it names the three compared parts and does not say "unchanged".

**Required change 3: the label and hover claim a morning catalyst and a currency the row does not have.** "Morning catalyst" reads as this morning's catalyst, and the label travels without its hover (stored copies, exports). The row shows the catalyst in a later version of the research, not an event of the morning. Label **"Catalyst in the morning research"**; hover: "The catalyst in the research the {morning ET} checkpoint used, shown only when that research was written after the research the {evening ET} recap used." ("As of the checkpoint" borrows the product's currency word, "as of", for a selection of versions; the Written line carries the date.)

**Passes.** Showing the row only for `NEWER`, hidden for `SAME_VERSION`, `NO_MORNING_RESEARCH`, `UNKNOWN`, `NOT_YET` and `NO_EVENING_CALL` (the Morning check row already speaks for the shared causes, no engine constant reaches a reader, and a hidden row claims nothing; nothing else on the surface may say "no morning update" for it); the Written line "Research written {date and time} ET." from `generatedAt` (7g: dated to its version); nothing says "cited" or "sourced"; the summary is the dossier's own text under the dossier's existing guardrails; the changed form prints the summary and the grade and status in the board's words.

## 12. The recap footnotes (Marketing `b3235fe1`, `docs/marketing/report-footnotes-words-2026-10-06.md`, v6 step 4), fourth tenure, 2026-10-06: NOT READY, two required changes; Marketing's two questions answered from the code

*Read: the file whole at `b3235fe1` (local main, not on origin when read) and, in Research's `3bfa667a`, `lib/desk/reports/narrative.ts` (the split-check header comment, `readRecapCounts`, `narrativeFootnotes`) and `contract.ts:188-203`. Marketing stayed blind to the code by rule; I am not under that rule for a claims read, and the answers below are from the code.*

**Marketing's question 2: what "traded" means for `noClose`.** `noClose` counts rows with `rth_final_close` null, whose latest price is inside the price range and whose `cumulative_volume` is above zero. So "traded" is any day volume at all (the desk's cumulative volume, which can include trading outside the regular session), not a volume floor and not necessarily a regular-session trade; "no close" is no regular-session close (`rth_final_close`). The recap's counts are regular-session counts. The sentence "{N} names traded but have no close, so they are not in these counts" is true; "close" should say which close.

**Marketing's question 1: which counts, and why a row is left out.** The split check leaves a row out of the breadth counts (eligible, traded, up, down), the 50 percent movers, "gave back" and "kept half" (the contract comment: F1, F6, F7, F15). A row is left out when its whole regular session trades 1.8 times or more away from its prior close, at a standard split ratio or with a split factor change in its daily bars near the date, because the stored prior close can be the pre-split close (a 1-for-5 would read as a fivefold gain). The code's own comment says a genuine gap within 5 percent of a standard ratio is left out too, "the cost is one name missing from a count, stated in the footnote". So the footnote is where a real mover left out is disclosed, and Marketing's words as drafted do not say it.

**Required change 1: footnote 2 states the cost the code says it states.** "{N} rows were left out of these counts by the desk's split check" is true and opaque: it does not say why, and it does not say that a real move can be among them. Say: "{N} rows were left out of these counts because the price move looked like a stock split. The check can also leave out a real move of the same size." Singular: "1 row was left out of these counts because the price move looked like a stock split. The check can also leave out a real move of the same size." Null stays "How many rows the desk's split check left out is unknown."

**Required change 2: a zero must print, because absence is ambiguous.** Marketing's rule is that a zero prints nothing and a missing footnote on a report that carries the field means zero. A report from before step 4 also prints nothing, and on those reports the counts were not split-checked at all, so a reader cannot tell "none were left out" from "this report predates the check" (a failed check returning the plausible answer). The contract comment says both counts are "Never hidden". Print the zero: "No names traded without a regular-session close." and "No rows were left out of these counts by the split check." Then only a report from before step 4 has no footnote, and that absence claims nothing.

**Footnote 1 precision (required with change 2's wording pass).** Use "regular-session close": "{N} names traded but have no regular-session close, so they are not in these counts." Singular "1 name traded but has no regular-session close, so it is not in these counts." Null "How many names traded without a regular-session close is unknown."

**Passes.** No clock; null prints as unknown and never as zero; the footnotes are plain text with no source claim; "these counts" is acceptable if Design places the footnotes directly under the recap counts they qualify (Design confirms); the recap-only scope.


## 12a. The recap footnotes v2 (Marketing `bc8820db`), fourth tenure, 2026-10-06: NOT READY, three required changes (two are 12's, not yet applied)

*Read: the file at `bc8820db` (local main, not on origin when read) against Research's `3bfa667a`: `isSplitRow` (narrative.ts:228-237), `readRecapCounts`, `recapNamed` and `narrativeFacts`. Marketing's words crossed 12 (the hold); the points below that repeat 12 are marked.*

**Verified against the code.** The split check's trigger is a jump (the session's low above 1.8 times the prior close, or its high below the prior close over 1.8) and either a close-to-prior ratio near a standard split ratio or a split-factor change near the date in the stored bars; it is not a verified split. A row it leaves out is dropped before the breadth counts (eligible, traded, up, down), the 50 percent movers, "gave back" and "held half", and the named recap leaders (`recapNamed` takes the counted movers); the fresh-news count and the covered names come from the covered facts and the research and are not affected. So "the move counts and the named leaders" names the right populations, and "had volume" (any day volume, which may or may not include pre-market) is the right word where "traded in the regular session" would claim more than the code establishes.

**Required change 1: footnote 2 drops the cost the code says it states.** "...because the desk could not verify their prior close" is true and does not tell a reader that a real mover can be among the rows left out (the code's own comment: a genuine gap within 5 percent of a standard ratio is left out too, "the cost is one name missing from a count, stated in the footnote"). Add: "A real move of the same size can be left out too." Footnote 2 then reads "{N} rows were left out of the move counts and the named leaders because the desk could not verify their prior close. A real move of the same size can be left out too."; singular "1 row was left out ... its prior close. A real move of the same size can be left out too."

**Required change 2 (12): print the zeros.** v2 keeps "a zero count prints nothing". A report from before step 4 also prints nothing and was never split-checked, so a reader cannot tell "none were left out" from "this report predates the check"; the contract comment calls both counts "Never hidden". Print: "No names had volume but no recorded close." and "No rows were left out of the move counts and the named leaders for an unverified prior close." Only a report from before step 4 then has no footnote.

**Required change 3: footnote 1 says "the counts" and "close" loosely.** "...so they are not in the counts" reads as every count in the recap, including the fresh-news count and the covered names, which the row is still in. Say "the move counts", as footnote 2 does, and "recorded regular-session close" (the missing value is `rth_final_close`): "{N} names had volume but no recorded regular-session close, so they are not in the move counts."; singular "1 name had volume but no recorded regular-session close, so it is not in the move counts."; null "How many names had volume but no recorded regular-session close is unknown."

**Passes.** No clock; null prints as unknown and never as zero; plain text with no source claim; no split claim and no ratio clause (the second trigger path has no ratio match); "had volume".

## 13. The DecisionCells badge "no catalyst found in our sources" (Marketing `0b3b473f`, last section of `docs/marketing/pm-catalyst-words-2026-10-06.md`), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06: PASS

*Read: `components/DecisionCells.tsx` 453-473 (the badge and its hover), `lib/dashboard/why.ts` 370-377 and the `code` returned at its foot, `lib/research/interpret/guardrails.ts` 420-428 (the NONE_FOUND coverage guardrail), `lib/desk/reports/check.ts` 138-144 and 356 (the admitted absence forms), and Marketing's section at `0b3b473f` (on local main, read from there). Not read: the OLD badge (a separate code, not in this read).*

**What fires it, verified.** The why-code `NO_FRESH_CATALYST` is returned only when `nothingFound` is true: a dossier catalyst exists, `plausiblyExplainsMove` is not true, and `catalystStatus === 'NONE_FOUND'` (`why.ts:377`). It is not a freshness claim despite the code's name, and no UNKNOWN status reaches it, so the badge never says "found nothing" for an analysis that reached no conclusion. At write time a NONE_FOUND is downgraded to UNKNOWN whenever coverage is incomplete (`guardrails.ts:420`, `CATALYST_NONE_REQUIRES_COVERAGE`), so the status itself already means "an adequate search located nothing" (I did not check whether any stored dossier predates that guardrail). Marketing's reading of the code is right.

**PASS: badge "no catalyst found in our sources", hover unchanged ("The desk searched its sources and found no catalyst for this move.").** It is the admitted form the report checker supports for NONE_FOUND (`check.ts:356`) and the same word the board prints at `language.ts:31` after 11b, so the two surfaces say one thing; the frame ("in our sources") is on the label, which travels without its hover; the hover names the search and not the world. No em dash (bytes and entity form, 0).

**The fallback, ruled now so Design does not wait a round if the width fails: "no catalyst in our sources" passes.** The checker's own pattern admits it (`check.ts:144`, the lookahead `(?! in our sources)`, after run 1 struck the true "we found no catalyst in our sources"); it keeps the noun and the frame and drops only "found". **"none in our sources" does not pass:** with no noun it reads as an answer to nothing when the badge is read away from its column, and it is the shape the standing rule on bare absence exists to prevent. Design measures the width first and sends it back; Marketing words nothing further.

**Recorded, not required.** The badge is a present-tense label on a stored dossier: a release published after the dossier's search is picked up by the news re-check, which covers the current top 12 with a 24-hour look-back (`recheck.ts`; I did not read the other re-look routes), so a name outside that set can wear the badge while news it has not looked at exists. The label says "our sources", which is true of what was searched; the hover says "searched", past tense. Nothing to change in the words; it is the reason no other form here may drop the frame.


## 14. Two v6 narrative writer-prompt lines (Research `ed4cbede`, `lib/desk/reports/writerV6.ts` NARRATIVE_RULES lines 186-187, deterministic checks in `check.ts`), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06: NOT READY, one required change

*Read: the commit on branch `research/reports-v6` (not on origin when read): the two lines, `namedClauses` and `intradaySheet`/`recapSheet` in the same file (what the model is handed), and the new `UNFRAMED_PERCENT` and `CAUSAL_WORDING` rules in `check.ts` (MOVE, FRAME, CAUSAL, `causalIn`, `wordingFindings`). Not read: the tests and mutants, the comparison result document, and any run (none has happened).*

**Required change: the line 1 example is copyable onto a name it is false of.** `("up 68 percent from the prior close, with fresh news found in our sources")` sits in a prompt the model reads for every name, and the per-name news fact is not always fresh: `namedClauses` hands the model `news: {NEWS_WORDS[status]}` ("fresh news found in our sources", "only older or secondary news found in our sources", "no catalyst found in our sources", "the sources conflict", "the research could not tell", or "not researched yet"). The figure is bounded, because a copied 68 that is not a given number is struck by the number check; the clause "fresh news found in our sources" is not, and only the model checker could catch it on a name whose status is not FRESH_FOUND. This is read 7a's class (an example tested against the rules and not against being copied). Use placeholders for both, and tie the news clause to the given words: `("{ticker} is up {percent} percent from the prior close, with {the news words given for that name}")`.

**Verified, passes.** The wording is the desk's own side-by-side rule (a catalyst is news found, never a cause, line 185) made concrete; "with" is the approved joiner and a connective after it is inside the news by design; the frames "from the prior close", "above the prior close", "after the close" and "after hours" are the product's own words and match the rule that after-hours figures are labelled; the checks strike by rule what the prompt forbids, and say in their own comment that a "%" form is not read and that one frame per clause admits a second unframed move.

**Recommended, not required.** (a) **The sheet hands the model a phrase line 2 calls unframed.** `recapSheet` line 122 gives "ran to {n} percent above the prior close and closed up {m} percent on the day"; line 2 says each move carries its own frame, and "on the day" is not one (the check admits it as a causal frame, not as a percent frame, and passes the clause only because the first move carries "the prior close"). Have the sheet say "closed up {m} percent from the prior close" (the close move is measured from the prior close, as the next line's "closed at or below the prior close" shows). (b) **"after" in line 1 meets "after hours" in line 2.** The check handles it (`FRAME_AT`); the prompt does not say so, and a model told never to use "after" next to a move may drop the after-hours frame. Add "(the frames 'after the close' and 'after hours' are not this)". (c) **The forbidden list is closed and so is the check's.** "as", "in response to", "in the wake of", "on the back of" and "behind" join a move to news as cause and are in neither list (line 185 has "behind the move" only as a phrase). The side-by-side principle carries them in the prompt, and nothing strikes them. If the list grows, grow the check's `CAUSAL` with it, or the prompt will forbid what nothing finds. (d) **The check reads a cause only after a move.** "After the filing, it ran 68 percent from the prior close" is not struck; line 1 says "between a move and its news" in either order, so state the limit in the check's comment.

**What this read does not settle.** "From the prior close" is the stored prior close, which the desk has shown differs from the official close on most measured names (typically under 1 percent, up to 6): the lines pass as wording, and a published sentence is true of the stored close only after Ops's official-close fix, which is the hold Research already names.


**14a. The placeholder form (Research `4d3c3b36`, branch `research/reports-v6`, not on origin when read), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06: 14's required change applied; one new required word, one recommended**

*Read: the diff of `4d3c3b36` against `ed4cbede` (`writerV6.ts` line 186 and the named-mover sheet line, `check.ts` CAUSAL and its limits comment). Searched `writerV6.ts` for a tense rule and `check.ts` for any handling of a literal brace: none in either.*

**14's required change: applied.** The example is `{ticker} is up {percent} percent from the prior close, with {the news words given for that name}`, so no news wording is copyable onto a name it is false of. Recommended (a) to (d): all applied as stated ("closed up N percent from the prior close" in the recap sheet; the frames sentence; "in response to", "in the wake of" and "on the back of" in the prompt, the first two in `CAUSAL` and the third caught by "on"; the limits in the check's comment). "As" is not added, and the reason (it would strike "as of" and "such as") is right.

**Required: "is up" states the present, and the prompt has no tense rule.** The recap is a premarket report about a session that has closed (`NARRATIVE_RULES`: "Recap report (premarket)"), and the sheet hands the model "closed up N percent from the prior close". A copy of the example's "is up" on a recap says the name is up that much now, at a time when the desk has not measured it. The 14 example had no verb. Take the verb out of the example: `({ticker}, up {percent} percent from the prior close, with {the news words given for that name})`, or give the model one of the two tenses by report kind.

**Recommended: a copied placeholder shows in the published text.** Nothing in `check.ts` strikes a literal `{` in a narrative, and a copied `{percent}` carries no digit for the number check; only the model checker would see it. A one-line deterministic strike on `{` or `}` in the narrative costs nothing, and the Warning Shot's build has the same rule for its example (a copied brace is removed).

**Recorded.** "From the prior close" in a recap means the close before the recapped session, and a reader at premarket can take it as last night's close. The words pass because the report's header carries the session's date (not read here); if any narrative is shown without its header, it needs the date.


**14b. PASS at Research `dcba259c` (branch `research/reports-v6`, not on origin when read), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06.** Read: the diff against `4d3c3b36`. The example reads ("{ticker}, up {percent} percent from the prior close, with {the news words given for that name}"), no verb, so it states no tense; a literal `{` or `}` in the narrative is struck by rule (`FORBIDDEN_WORDING`, "a template placeholder copied from the prompt"), placed beside `wordingFindings` on the same narrative text. 14 and 14a are closed. Still open and not this seat's to close: the recap's "from the prior close" holds only while the report header carries the session date (neither Research nor this seat has read the header), and "from the prior close" is the stored close until Ops's official-close fix. No em dash (bytes, 0).


## 15. Morning check NOT_YET, second cause: the checkpoint completed but did not read the name (Marketing `57f28174`, last section of `docs/marketing/warning-shot-confirmation-words-2026-10-06.md`), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06: PASS with two build conditions

*Read: the section at `57f28174` (on local main), `lib/desk/morningRead.ts` on `research/confirmation` (`confirmationOf`, `morningReadOf`, `MORNING_TYPES`), and spec section 4 (the states and their order). Not read: Design's render, and `readAssessmentTickers`.*

**The string.** Face "No morning check yet" (unchanged); detail **"This name was not among the names the {morning ET} checkpoint read."**; no Compared line; "Evening recap: {evening ET}." may print alone. **PASS.** It states only the stored fact: `morningReadOf` takes the latest COMPLETE morning checkpoint at or before `at`, and the detail says the name is not in that checkpoint's rows (`morningTickers`). It claims nothing about why, makes no promise, and the old detail ("No morning checkpoint has completed for this date.") is false in this cause, as Marketing says. `{morning ET}` is the checkpoint's `completed_at` in ET, the token 7 and 11 already passed.

**Build condition 1: the detail prints only for a name on the completed evening recap.** The code does not give Design that fact. `byTicker` omits a name absent from the evening recap, `morningTickers` lists the morning rows, and the evening rows are not exported, so "absent from byTicker and from `morningTickers`" is true of two different names: one on the evening recap (the NOT_YET state, which prints this detail) and one on neither (NO_EVENING_CALL, which spec section 4 says prints nothing, because a name new this morning is not unconfirmed). Printed for the second, the string is literally true and the face "No morning check yet" is wrong. Design needs the evening membership from Research (the rows `morningReadOf` already reads) before the string is built; it must not infer it from the two lists.

**Build condition 2: no evening recap, no detail.** `morningReadOf` returns an empty `byTicker` when either checkpoint is missing. A completed morning checkpoint with no completed evening recap is the case Integration ruled renders "Unknown" (its 2026-10-06 message, item 2, for the Confirmation build; I did not read the ruling's own turn), not this one; the detail must not print there, because "not among the names the checkpoint read" would be said of every name on the board.

**Recorded, not required: "yet" has a last chance.** Marketing's reason for keeping "yet" (a later checkpoint may include the name) is true only while a morning checkpoint can still complete: `MORNING_TYPES` are PREMARKET_0625, PREMARKET_0645, PREMARKET_0700 and OPEN_SPIKE_ASSESSMENT, so after the last of them has completed for the day nothing can change it. The face was passed in 7 and the detail does not promise anything, so nothing changes; it is the reason the detail, not the face, has to carry the cause. I did not read when OPEN_SPIKE_ASSESSMENT runs.


## 16. The "moving" label for a row that is also RVOL-ranked (Marketing `02c44d2d`, `docs/marketing/moving-label-words-2026-10-06.md`; Design's request, Integration 31st's defect fix), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06: NOT READY, one required change

*Read: the file at `02c44d2d` (final caption section; it supersedes section 2 and the two-outcome plan) and, at tag `design/warning-shot-candidate-ce4e297d`, `lib/market/boardMovers.ts` (the rule and its header), `lib/market/readModel.ts` 641-651 (how mover rows are appended) and `lib/dashboard/view.ts` `movingNote` and `movingCaption`. Not read: the fix itself (it is not built), Ops's field, and the 2026-10-01 rulings' own turns.*

**Required change: "on the board for its move" states a cause that is false of a ranked row.** The rule is additive: the board keeps its top 50 by RVOL and ADDS up to 15 names "not already on it" that meet the floors (`boardMovers.ts` header; `addBoardMovers` skips any ticker already on the board). A ranked mover is on the board because it is ranked; its move put nothing on the board. So the row hover's "Also on the board for its move." and the caption's "{N} names are on the board for a large move" claim a reason for presence that, for IPDN (rank 14, inside the top 50), is the rank. Marketing's note says the hover "says nothing about which one put the row there"; the word "for" says it. State what the name did, not why it is there. Row: "Also a mover. Up {x}% from the prior close on {n} shares since 04:00 ET. RVOL rank {r}." Caption: a form that names the move and not the reason for the place (my wording here is an example only; "whether or not they are also in the RVOL ranking" stays). Marketing words the final form; the constraint is that neither string says the move is why a ranked row is on the board. The as-built hover for a mover outside the ranking ("On the board for its move, not its RVOL rank") is true of that row and stays.

**Passes.** (a) The restored Top N clause, "and Top 12, 25 or 50 can leave some of them off": true of both kinds today (unranked movers are appended after the 50 ranked rows, so even Top 50 leaves them off; a ranked mover at rank 14 is cut by Top 12), and "some of them" is exact; it comes out only in the build that shows movers past Top N, as Marketing says. (b) "Under the Rank and RVOL sorts, names outside the RVOL ranking come after the ranked names": the code appends mover rows after the ranked ones and every other sort places them by its own value (his ruling, quoted in the `boardMovers.ts` header). (c) "Up {x}% from the prior close on {n} shares since 04:00 ET": the rule's own two numbers, the row's, with no floor quoted. (d) "A large move" names no threshold and is the board's own "big movers" vocabulary; the floors stay server config.

**Build condition (Design and Ops, not words): the label and its numbers must come from one read.** Present tense ("Up {x}%", "{N} names are moving") is earned for the as-built label because `readModel` re-selects movers on every read from the current move. Marketing says the ranked-row hover ships "with Ops's field". If that field is stored and not recomputed at read time, a ranked row can wear "Also a mover. Up 70%" after the move has fallen under the floor. The label, {x}, {n} and the caption count must all be computed from the current demand state at read time, as the unranked label is.

**Recorded.** "From the prior close" is the stored close until Ops's official-close fix (the same hold as elsewhere); it is the board's existing word and is not changed here.


**16a. Read 16 applied (Marketing `dc8fa10d`, last section of `docs/marketing/moving-label-words-2026-10-06.md`), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06: the row hover PASSES; the caption is NOT READY on one required change**

Design's report that `moving` is recomputed from the current move on every read (`readModel.ts`, `getMarketSnapshot` calling `selectMovers` and `addBoardMovers` each time) is Design's statement, not read here; it satisfies 16's build condition if the field is set in that path.*

**Required change to the caption: it lost its scope, and the count now reads as every large move.** "{N} names made a large move from the prior close" has no "on the board" and no tie to the label. The rule counts only names in the served price band, on at least the volume floor, at or above the move floor, and at most 15 added unranked movers (`selectMovers`, `addBoardMovers`); a name up as much on too few shares, outside the band, or past the cap is not counted. So N is "the names the board marks moving", and the sentence as written says it is the names that made a large move. This follows from dropping "on the board" in 16's repair; the repair was right about cause and the scope has to come back. Say what N counts: for example "{N} names on the board are marked moving: each made a large move from the prior close, whether or not it is also in the RVOL ranking." (plural "they are ... each made"; singular "1 name on the board is marked moving: it made a large move ..."). The label is the rule's, so "marked moving" claims the rule fired and not why a row is on the board. The rest of the caption (ordering, the restored Top N clause, "any other sort") is unchanged and passes as in 16.


**16b. PASS at Marketing `493c8e33`, fifth tenure, 2026-10-06.** The caption reads "{N} names on the board are marked moving: each made a large move from the prior close, whether or not it is also in the RVOL ranking. Under the Rank and RVOL sorts, names outside the RVOL ranking come after the ranked names, and Top 12, 25 or 50 can leave some of them off. Any other sort places every name by its own value." (singular "1 name on the board is marked moving: it made a large move ... can leave it off"). N is scoped to the label, the row hover passed in 16a, and 16 and 16a are closed. Still Design's and Ops's, not words: the label and its numbers computed at read time (Design reports `getMarketSnapshot` calls `selectMovers` and `addBoardMovers` on every read; not read here).


## 17. The Discovery filter banner (Marketing `db6857de`, `docs/marketing/filter-banner-words-2026-10-06.md`, supersedes `daba7c92`; Design's request after IPDN), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06: NOT READY, three required changes

*Read: the file whole at `db6857de`; at tag `design/warning-shot-candidate-ce4e297d`, `lib/dashboard/view.ts` (`isFilterActive`, `rowMatchesFilters`), `components/FilterBar.tsx` (group and option labels), `components/DashboardShell.tsx` 268-296 and 423; `lib/market/boardMovers.ts` for the mover set (read in 16). The banner is not built.*

**Passes.** All six filters named, each by the label the reader sees ("Search: {text}", "Setup grade", "Cycle", "Structure", "Freshness", "Grade"), a group at "All" or an empty search not listed (`isFilterActive` counts exactly those six); the Setup grade values as the stored-to-letter mapping read 6 passed ("D" for AVOID, "Not graded"); "Clear filters"; the name hover "up {x}% from the prior close on {n} shares since 04:00 ET" (the row's own numbers, no rule number, no cause, no tradability, as in the moving hover); and "Moving" as the board's own label word for the same set. The "Grade" ambiguity Marketing raises is real and its recommendation is right: relabel the chip group "Catalyst grade" in the same build (`rowMatchesFilters` compares `why.grade`, the letter the Why cell renders), so chip and banner say one thing; the banner must not print a bare "Grade" beside "Setup grade".

**Required change 1: "Showing {X} of {Y}" has no stated X, and the code I read makes X the filter-pass count.** `DashboardShell.tsx` 273-275 builds `discoveryRows` as `merged.discovery` filtered by `rowMatchesFilters`, and 423 passes `visibleCount={discoveryRows.length}`. That is every name that passes, which is not the number of rows on screen if the Top 12, 25 or 50 control cuts the table afterwards; I could not find in `components` where that cut is applied (the only `discoveryRank` read is the event feed's, line 292), so I cannot say whether `merged.discovery` is already the Top N cut or the full board. "Showing 7 of 31 names" is true only if 7 rows are on screen, and the hover's "{Y} is every name on the board before these filters, not just the Top 12, 25 or 50 shown" is true only if Y is counted over the full board and not over rows that were already cut. Design names the two arrays X and Y are counted from. If X is the pass count and Top N can still cut the table, say "{X} of {Y} names match" (no "Showing"); if X is the rendered rows, keep "Showing". Y is "every name on the board" only if the array includes the appended movers.

**Required change 2: line 2's set is "movers the filters hide", and two builds can make it wrong.** (a) It must list names that fail the filters (`rowMatchesFilters` false), not names the Top N control cuts while they pass: a passing name cut by Top 12 is not hidden by a filter, and "these filters hide" would be false of it. (b) The label today marks only movers outside the ranking (`boardReason: 'MOVER'` is set by `addBoardMovers`, which skips ranked names); the ranked-mover fix (reads 16 to 16b) is what makes an IPDN, rank 14 and up 70 percent, part of the set. Before that fix ships, line 2 would not list the very case it was designed for, and it would say "Moving names these filters hide" of a set that leaves out a moving name. The banner ships with or after the label fix, or its words say less.

**Required change 3: the down forms cannot occur and contradict "the same set".** The banner words "ABCD -41%" and a hover "down {x}%". The mover rule selects names at or above a positive floor ("moving at least FLOOR (+10%)", `boardMovers.ts`; `selectMovers` keeps `percentChange >= minPct`), so no name in the set is down. A line that shows a minus is either not the moving set or is unreachable copy. Remove the down forms. (The as-built `movingNote` carries a defensive "Down"; that is code, not words.)

**Recorded, not required.** (a) The percent in line 2 ("{TICKER} +70%") carries its frame only in the hover; the board's own change column is labelled by session ("Gap", "Change from the most recent session close", `view.ts` near 549), so Design confirms the line-2 number is the same quantity as the moving hover's `percentChange`, and that a reader comparing it with the column sees the same figure. (b) "From the prior close" is the stored close until Ops's official-close fix, as everywhere else.

## 18. Movers past Top N: the caption and the banner count hover (Marketing `76110b1b`, `moving-label-words-2026-10-06.md` and `filter-banner-words-2026-10-06.md`), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06: NOT READY, two required changes, one correction to my own 16b

*His ruling, verified at his own turn: an `enqueue` record at 2026-10-06T14:41:43.039Z in session `00b2db8f` carries "yes show movers past top n"; the sentence after it in that record ("yes reset") was addressed to that seat and is not carried here. Read: the two new sections at `76110b1b` (no em dash, bytes, 0); at tag `design/warning-shot-candidate-ce4e297d`, `components/DiscoveryBoard.tsx` 329-330 and 422 (the Top N slice, and `movingCaption(rows)`) and `DashboardShell.tsx` 273-276 and 528 (`rows` is `discoveryRows`, the filtered rows). Not read: the append build, which does not exist yet.*

**Correction to my 16b (the caption I passed): it counts the filtered rows, and its scope sentence was passed without reading its call site.** `DiscoveryBoard.tsx:422` calls `movingCaption(rows)`, and `DashboardShell.tsx:528` passes `rows={discoveryRows}`, which is `merged.discovery` after `rowMatchesFilters`. So with a filter on, "{N} names on the board are marked moving" counts only the movers that pass the filters; the ones the filters hide are on the board and are not in N, and the banner above lists them. I passed "on the board" in 16a and 16b on the label's scope and did not open what the count is taken over. **Required: N and the words name the same set.** Either count the movers before the filters (the board's movers, matching "on the board", and matching the banner's Y), or say what N counts when a filter is on ("{N} names that match these filters are marked moving"). Design chooses; the caption string follows. This applies to today's live caption as well as the append build.

**Required: the new count line collides with the caption's own placeholder.** "{N} names on the board are marked moving ... Top {N} also shows {k} moving name(s) outside it." uses {N} for the count of movers in the first sentence and for the Top setting in the second. A build that binds one value to both prints "Top 3 also shows 2 moving names outside it". Name the second `{top}`. The line prints only when k is above zero ("Top 12 also shows 0 moving names outside it" is true and says nothing), k comes from the build's own count, and with a filter on k counts the movers that pass the filters, since the filters run before the slice.

**Passes, conditional on the build.** The caption without the clause "and Top 12, 25 or 50 can leave some of them off" is true only once movers are shown past Top N, as Marketing says; until the append ships the live caption keeps the clause. The banner hover "{Y} is every name on the board before these filters. {X} match; the table shows the first 12, 25 or 50 of them, plus any moving names among the rest." is true if the appended movers are subject to the filters and are shown after the slice; Design confirms from the build, and until the append ships the passed "at most" hover stays.


**18a. PASS at Marketing `8d00a97a` (`moving-label-words-2026-10-06.md`, no em dash, bytes and entity form, 0), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06.** The caption has two forms, one per what Design builds: counted before the filters, "{N} names on the board are marked moving: each made a large move from the prior close, whether or not it is also in the RVOL ranking. ..."; or counted over the filtered rows, with a filter on "{N} names that match these filters are marked moving: ..." (singular "1 name that matches these filters is marked moving: it made ...") and with no filter the "on the board" form. Each states what its N counts, which is the point of 18. The count line is "Top {top} also shows {k} moving name(s) outside it.", {top} 12, 25 or 50, printed only when k is above zero, k from the build's own count (with a filter on, the movers that pass); {N} stays the caption's mover count. The two conditional passes in 18 stand (the caption without the Top N clause; the banner hover "the first 12, 25 or 50 of them, plus any moving names among the rest"), true once the append ships and the appended movers are subject to the filters. 18 is closed. Build condition for Design: the form built must be the one whose count matches its words (before the filters with "on the board", or over the filtered rows with "match these filters"), never a mix.


**18b. The banner count hover with a limit (Marketing `3a82afa8`), and the built caption, count line, row hover and basis pin (Design `e0bdded4`), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06: PASS**
