## 20. Do the landing sentence and the float page contradict? And the counts strip hint for the mover release (Integration's question; Marketing `970acacd`), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06

*Read: `app/page.tsx` 280 at origin ("No filing reports the current number of tradable shares."), `docs/public/are-float-quotes-real.md` at `0ca637aa` ("We know of no SEC filing that requires a count of floating shares", and the paragraph around it), and my own 4 (item 4) on the Global Cash Access S-1 and on this landing sentence.*

**No contradiction; a reader can hold both, and the page is the stronger sentence.** They are different claims. The page says no filing we know of REQUIRES a count of floating shares (a requirement, hedged by "we know of", checked at source in 5: Rule 12b-2's public float is a dollar value) and goes on in the next paragraphs to say "a float number on a quote page is an estimate someone computed, unless a filing happens to state it", so the page itself allows that a filing can state a count. The landing sentence says no filing REPORTS the CURRENT number of tradable shares; the page uses the same word the same way ("a dollar value on one past date, not a current count of tradable shares"). The float page sentence is true as written, and it is not a blocker for tonight.

**The landing sentence is the one with a weakness, and it is his.** It is a flat "No filing" universal that stands on one word. I hold a counterexample I opened: Global Cash Access Holdings' 2006 S-1 states "approximately 30,130,351 shares ... will be freely tradable without restriction under the Securities Act, unless held by our 'affiliates'", a point-in-time count at an offering (4, item 4). "Current" is what keeps the sentence true of it, and a reader may not weigh that word. I cleared the sentence in 4 on that one word; the precondition is stated here. A one-line option for him, not urgent and not for tonight: **"No filing we know of reports the current number of tradable shares."** It matches the page's own hedge, costs nothing, and removes the dependence on "current". If it is put to him, the wording is his to accept or leave.

**The counts strip hint for the mover release (Marketing `970acacd`): PASS, conditional on the append build.** "Names in the ranking at the time shown. The board shows the first 12, 25 or 50 of them, plus moving names outside them, up to a limit." True once `visibleWithMovers` ships: the first N of the sorted rows, then the moving rows below the line, at most 15, a ranked mover cut below N and an unranked one alike; the cap number stays out of the copy. It replaces the strip's current hint in the same build.


## 21. The float page review, Web's smaller items 3 to 8 read against the sources (`docs/web/2026-10-06-float-page-review-and-tooltips.md` at `bdb171f7`, section 2), fifth tenure, 2026-10-06 (night): four wording changes, and one blind spot found in my own earlier pass

*Asked by Integration 31st after his "yeah let's fix it." (2026-10-07T00:02:35.703Z, Web's chat, transcript 2fc25a39, verified by Integration). Sources opened tonight: 17 CFR 230.424(b) and 240.12b-2 (law.cornell.edu, the text read from the page, not a summary); the SEC Form 10-K (sec.gov/files/form10-k.pdf, SEC 1673 (02-25), cover page, read with pymupdf; its ligature in "affiliates" defeats a plain search for "affiliates") and Form 8-K (sec.gov/files/form8-k.pdf, Item 3.02, read with pymupdf). Not read tonight: Web's points 1, 2 and 9 (the central claim and the cell), which wait on Research's answer to his cell question and are the next read; Rule 13d-3's text; any built check.*

**Item 6, the 10-K cover requirement: Web is right.** The requirement is Form 10-K's cover page: "State the aggregate market value of the voting and non-voting common equity held by non-affiliates computed by reference to the price at which the common equity was last sold, or the average bid and asked price of such common equity, as of the last business day of the registrant's most recently completed second fiscal quarter", with a Note allowing "assumptions reasonable under the circumstances" where affiliate status cannot be settled without unreasonable effort. Rule 12b-2 does not impose it. It uses the term "public float" in the smaller reporting company definition, measured "as of the last business day of the issuer's most recently completed second fiscal quarter", and gives the same date to the accelerated filer tests. The page's "(Rule 12b-2)" after the cover sentence attributes the requirement to the wrong source. A true form: "on the cover of Form 10-K, the dollar value of the common equity held by non-affiliates, as of the last business day of the second fiscal quarter (the SEC's rules call this the public float, Rule 12b-2)". The Note is also worth a clause: the cover figure can rest on stated assumptions, which supports the page's own "an estimate someone computed".

**Item 7, Item 3.02 aggregation: Web is right; this is a precision, not a required change.** Form 8-K Item 3.02(b): no report is needed if the securities sold, "in the aggregate since its last report filed under this Item 3.02 or its last periodic report, whichever is more recent", are less than 1 percent of the shares outstanding of the class (5 percent for a smaller reporting company); Instruction 1: "the number of shares outstanding" is the actual shares of the class and excludes outstanding convertibles. Item 3.02(a): the report is due within four business days after the company enters an enforceable agreement to sell, or after closing if there is none, so a report can precede the issuance. "Counted in total since the last report" is short for it; say "counted in total since the company's last such report or last periodic report, whichever is more recent". It stays on the safe side either way (the desk looks at more than the sentence says).

**Items 3 and 4, the 424B5 gloss and the exclusion sentence: two sentences are not true as written, and the fix is to name forms, not content.** Rule 424(b), read at the text: (b)(1) pricing information omitted in reliance on Rule 430A (the 424B1); (b)(2) a prospectus used in a PRIMARY offering of securities under Rule 415(a)(1)(x) or on a delayed basis under 415(a)(1)(vii) or (viii), disclosing pricing information omitted in reliance on Rule 430B (the 424B2); (b)(3) facts or events other than those, a substantive change from the last prospectus (the 424B3); (b)(4) both (b)(1) and (3) (the 424B4); (b)(5) both (b)(2) and (3) (the 424B5); (b)(7) a prospectus that identifies selling security holders and the amounts they sell (the 424B7). (a) The page's gloss "a final prospectus for an offering of shares (SEC form 424B1, 424B4 or 424B5)" is not true of every filing on those forms: Rule 424(b) says "securities", and a takedown of notes, preferred stock or warrants files on the same forms. The check is by form, so it refuses on those too (the safe direction), and the gloss should not say "shares". (b) Web's point that a 424B5 can be a resale is not established by the rule: a resale by identified selling holders is (b)(7) or (b)(3), and (b)(5) is a primary offering plus other changes. I have not opened an example of a resale filed as a 424B5; the sentence "Resale prospectuses ... do not trigger the check" is nevertheless the wrong shape, because it names a kind of document and the check tests a form. Say what is tested: "We look for two kinds of filing made on or after the count's date: a final prospectus for an offering of securities (SEC forms 424B1, 424B4 and 424B5), and a report of an unregistered sale of shares (Form 8-K item 3.02). We do not look at other forms, including 424B2, 424B3 and 424B7." That also answers item 4 (a resale document can still show potential issuance: the sentence now says what is screened and claims nothing about relevance), and the popover's clause ("no final prospectus for an offering of shares ... and no report of an unregistered sale of shares ... filed on or after the count date") takes the same "securities" change.

**A blind spot in my own pass, found tonight and not Web's: the page calls Form 424B2 a "resale prospectus or prospectus update", and by the rule it is a primary-offering form.** Rule 424(b)(2) is the form for a prospectus used in a primary offering on a delayed (shelf) basis, the same trigger as the 424B5 without the other changes. The v19 and v20 sentence "Resale prospectuses and prospectus updates (such as forms 424B2 and 424B3) do not trigger the check" (passed in 5s, 5t) therefore describes 424B2 as something it is not, and the check not triggering on it is a coverage gap, not a screening of an irrelevant form: a shelf takedown filed alone as a 424B2 is not seen. The measurement excluded 424B2 by its prereg ("424B3 (resale), 424B2, S-3 and S-1 filings alone are NOT events"), and I read 424(b) in 5q and described the paragraphs by what triggers them without noticing that (b)(2) is itself a primary-offering trigger. Whether equity takedowns on this board are ever filed as 424B2 is not measured by me. **Asked of Research:** count the 424B2 filings on or after the count date for the validation names (I wrote "and the board's names the submissions listings already hold", a premise I had not opened: Research answered that no listings are stored, `issuance-miss.ts` fetched them live, so the script `b2-count.ts` at `dad4a2f8` refetches the SEC submissions for both validation draws' produced names only, window count date to D, with a control that its 424B1, 424B4 and 424B5 hits equal the committed raw e1 name by name, and each 424B2 found is opened by hand), and either add 424B2 to the check's E1 or let the page state it as a blind spot ("a shelf takedown filed as a 424B2 is not looked at"). Until then the page must not call it a resale or an update.

**Item 5, an offering prospectus does not prove the offering closed: no change.** "When we find a flag or a filing showing that shares have been issued since the count, or may have been" already says "may have been".

**Item 8, 13D and 13G: no change.** The paragraph says what the desk does not subtract and why, and its example is a reconciliation failure (the combined beneficial totals exceeded the filed share count); it does not say the filings are useless. Rule 13d-3's text was not reopened tonight; the paragraph's two reasons (shares a holder may acquire within 60 days; shares whose voting or investment the holder controls) are the rule's own concepts as I know them, and I have not re-read them at source.

**Items 1, 2 and 9 are held** for Research's answer on the "at least", the conventional definition and the cell, then the claims read, then Marketing's words, then him (Integration's order).


**21a. Research's 424B2 count (`3bdf6a94`, `docs/research/float-contract/b2-count-result-2026-10-07.md`, raw `09b4ef0e`) and its question about 424B3, fifth tenure, 2026-10-07 (UTC).** *Read: the result file whole; Rule 424(b)(3) at the text (law.cornell.edu, read last night in read 21). Not read: the script, the raw file, any of the 53 filings.*

**The result, as stated:** 0 filings of form 424B2 in 60 name-checkpoints (59 tickers), the windows running from each count's as-of date to D; the control (the 424B1, 424B4 and 424B5 hits in each window equal the committed raw e1 name by name) passed 60 of 60 with 17 names showing a present hit, so the script saw filings that existed; the board's other names are not covered, because nothing on disk holds their listings, and a zero in 60 is not a rate. Research's recommendation, to add 424B2 to the I-D hit list, is consistent with the rule's text: Rule 424(b)(2) is the primary shelf-takedown form, the addition can only add refusals, and the page's description of 424B2 as a resale or an update is removed in Web's wording pass.

**Research's question: can a 424B3 carry a primary offering? Yes, by the rule's own text, so the 53 are worth opening.** Rule 424(b)(3) is "a form of prospectus that reflects facts or events other than those covered in paragraphs (b)(1), (2) and (6) of this section that constitute a substantive change from or addition to the information set forth in the last form of prospectus filed". It is a catch-all for any substantive change; it does not say resale, and it is not limited to supplements that attach a periodic report. So a 424B3 can accompany a primary offering that is not priced under Rule 430A or 430B (a continuous offering at a fixed price, an amended plan of distribution) and a registration whose shares the company has not yet issued. What the hand-opening has to separate, for each of the 53 (on 18 names): (1) a resale of shares already outstanding (adds none); (2) a resale registration of shares not yet issued (shares underlying warrants or convertibles, an equity-line purchase agreement): the company will issue them when the holder exercises, converts or puts, with no filing of its own, which is the page's existing "conversions or exercises" blind spot, now with a name and a date attached; (3) a primary offering by the company. Only (2) and (3) bear on the count. I have not opened any of the 53; the three-way split is my reading of what the form can carry, not a finding about these filings.

**21b. What the page may call 424B3 and 424B7 (Integration 31st's question, after the 424B2 ruling), fifth tenure, 2026-10-07 (UTC).** A 424B3 can carry a primary offering of the registrant's own securities by the rule's text (21a: Rule 424(b)(3) is any substantive change from the last prospectus, not limited to resale); no filing of that kind has been opened by anyone yet, and Research classifies the 53 by cover page before any code change. **The safest page wording names the forms and describes nothing:** "We do not look at other forms, including 424B3 and 424B7." If a gloss is wanted, the true ones are the rule's own: a 424B3 is "a prospectus filed for a substantive change from an earlier prospectus" (it can accompany a resale, a periodic report, or an offering), and a 424B7 is "a prospectus that names selling security holders and the amounts they sell" (Rule 424(b)(7): information about selling holders omitted from the registration statement in reliance on Rule 430B). Neither may be called "resale" without qualification: 424B7 is the resale-identification form, and 424B3 is not a resale form. If the classification finds primary offerings among the 53, 424B3 joins the check (or the page says a 424B3 filed for a primary offering is not looked at) and the page names the form either way.


**21c. Research's 424B3 classification (`de1bf078`, `docs/research/float-contract/b3-result-2026-10-07.md`) and what an equity-line put files, fifth tenure, 2026-10-07 (UTC).** *Read: the result file whole; and, opened at the SEC tonight (python with an identifying user agent, text read), four of the 53: C06 KITT (Nauticus Robotics, an Equity Purchase Facility), C39 CHGA (Change Agents, an ELOC with Hudson), C53 AIXC (a VWAP purchase agreement with Gold King Arthur) and C31 SHMD (a 424B3 supplement attaching a Form 6-K). Not read: the other 49, the hand classes `cfadf876`, the key's other entries, any company's own 8-K or 10-Q on a put.*

**The result: no code change for 424B3, and the page names it among the forms not looked at, with no description (21b).** 0 primary of 53, 39 class 2 (shares not yet issued), 8 class 1, 3 ADR forms, 3 undetermined; the blind control found exactly the three planted primaries; all 18 names were already refused by the dossier's issuance flag. Research's reading that a class 2 424B3 registers an instrument and does not report an issuance is right, and I agree it is the page's existing "conversions or exercises that file nothing" blind spot, not a sign that it was live.

**Research's question: what does an equity-line put file? From the four filings: nothing of its own that I could find, and the sale is described as an unregistered one.** KITT's prospectus says the company's offer and sale under the Facility Agreement and the commitment shares "are being made in reliance upon the exemptions from the registration requirements of the Securities Act pursuant to Section 4(a)(2) thereof and Rule 506(b) of Regulation D"; CHGA's says the put shares, the warrant and its shares "were, and will be, offered and sold to Hudson in reliance upon the exemption from registration provided by Section 4(a)(2)". AIXC's prospectus cites the agreement's signing as a Form 8-K (filed June 17, 2026). I searched the three equity-line prospectuses for "Form 8-K" and read the first three hits in each; none says a filing accompanies a put. That is a partial search, not a proof that none does. So, as these two prospectuses describe it, each put is a sale of equity securities in a transaction not registered under the Securities Act, which is Form 8-K Item 3.02's subject (AIXC's prospectus has no such sentence in the part I searched), and Item 3.02(b)'s thresholds (read in 21: aggregate since the last Item 3.02 report or periodic report, less than 1 percent of the class, or less than 5 percent for a smaller reporting company, needs no report) apply to the running total of puts. That makes an equity line one more way to issue shares with nothing filed, and the page's private-placement sentence already states the threshold; it does not name an equity line, and a reader would not connect the two. SHMD's supplement shows the other way a put surfaces: in the text it attaches (the Form 6-K's half-year results material) a table reads "Standby Equity Purchase Agreement up to $30.0m of which $9.0m have been utilized", reported with the periodic results, not at a put. I did not open any company's own 8-K or 10-Q on a put, so "usually reported in the periodic report" is not claimed.

**Required in the page's blind-spot sentence (Web words, Commercial passes):** name it. A true form: "sales of newly issued shares to an equity-line investor, who buys on the company's demand under an exemption from registration and resells under a registered prospectus: each such sale files nothing unless the running total since the company's last such report or last periodic report reaches the item 3.02 size." The words "usually" and "typically" stay out; the four filings do not measure how often.

**A second blind spot the SHMD filing shows, for Research to answer, not a finding about the cell:** SCHMID Group N.V. files Form 6-K as a foreign private issuer ("REPORT OF FOREIGN PRIVATE ISSUER PURSUANT TO RULE 13a-16 OR 15d-16", read in the attached report), and Item 3.02 is an item of Form 8-K, which a 6-K filer does not file. If a name that files 20-F and 6-K can reach a float cell, the check's second limb (an 8-K with item 3.02) has no counterpart for it, and the page's "a report of an unregistered sale of shares (Form 8-K item 3.02)" describes a report that company is not required to file. **Asked of Research:** can a 20-F and 6-K filer print a cell, and if so how does the count and the check treat it? I did not open the form text on this point (the Form 8-K PDF I read does not mention foreign private issuers), so the Form 8-K scope is stated from the filing in front of me, not from the form.

**One note on the classes, not a correction:** C31's own cover reads "Resale of up to 5,000,000 Ordinary Shares" by selling securityholders and attaches a 6-K; its class 2 rests on the base F-1 (registration 333-296006, the Yorkville SEPA), which I did not open.


**21d. Research's foreign-filer answer (`9c81079d`, `docs/research/float-contract/foreign-filer-cell-2026-10-07.md`), fifth tenure, 2026-10-07 (UTC): the page sentence is false for those names; option (a), refuse, is the one that keeps it true**

*Read: the result file whole; `lib/research/floatJob.ts` 134 at tip `0ca637aa` (the item 3.02 limb matches only forms `8-K` and `8-K/A`, as Research says); 17 CFR 240.3a12-3 at the text (law.cornell.edu). Not read: the name files behind the 29, the code that picks the count form, the Section 16(a) statute or the 2026 amendment release.*

**What the file says, as I read it.** The code has no foreign-filer rule, and the count is the cover value from any form, so a 20-F or 40-F filer can print a cell. In the two validation draws 29 of the 60 counts came from a 20-F, 20-F/A or 40-F, and the 90-day rule stopped 28 of them; SANG (a 40-F count, exactly 90 days old at D) was refused only by its split proof and would otherwise have printed the count itself with nothing subtracted. The offering-prospectus limb of the check still sees these companies' 424B filings; the unregistered-sale limb never runs on a 6-K filer. So "a report of an unregistered sale of shares (Form 8-K item 3.02)" in the page, the popover and the aria text states a check that does not run for those names.

**Verdict: option (a), refuse a count whose form is 20-F, 20-F/A, 40-F or 40-F/A, is the one that keeps the page true as written.** Option (b), print and say on the page that a 6-K filer's unregistered-sale report is not looked for, is also true if the page says it, but it prints a number whose second protection is absent, in the one place the page promises both. The cost in the sample is one name already refused. Words for the refusal are Marketing's; the constraint is that the stated reason is true of the cause, not "we did not check for issuance" (which is also true of a name whose 424B check did run) and not the existing `ISSUANCE_NOT_CHECKED` text if that means "no dossier": say what is the case, for example "the company files the forms of a foreign issuer (20-F, 40-F, 6-K), and we do not look in those for unregistered sales." The Cannot verify bullet on the page lists the reasons, so this one goes into that list in the same wording.

**Two limits, both Research's and both stated in its file.** The count's form is a proxy for "files 6-K, not 8-K": a foreign issuer that files domestic forms would carry a 10-Q count and is not caught, and nobody has measured how many. And the sample is two validation draws.

**What I opened that bears on the insider half, one line, not a finding.** Rule 3a12-3(b), as it stands at law.cornell.edu (amended at 91 FR 10333, 3 March 2026), exempts securities registered by a foreign private issuer from sections 14(a), 14(b), 14(c), 14(f), 16(b) and 16(c) of the Exchange Act, and does not exempt section 16(a), the reporting section. So "a foreign issuer's insiders do not file Forms 3, 4 and 5" is not something the page may say, and who must file for a foreign issuer I have not settled (Research's 1 holding in 29 is a measurement of what the validation recorded, not a statement of what is required). On such a name the number is close to the count, and the page's "we subtract the shares the company's insiders report holding directly" must not be read as describing it.

**C31 and C49, closed:** Research opened the base F-1 (C49, registration 333-296006, whose cover says the shares are ones "we may, in our discretion, elect to issue and sell to Yorkville" under the SEPA), so C31's class 2 rests on a filing that was read, and my note in 21c is answered.


**21e. Float draft v21 (Marketing `e097f5b9`, `docs/marketing/float-page-draft.md`; the three strings that answer 21c and 21d), fifth tenure, 2026-10-07 (UTC): NOT READY, two required changes, one dependency; the rest of v21 is v20 and is not the page to ship**

*Read: the diff of `e097f5b9` against its parent (no em dash, bytes and entity form, 0); 17 CFR 240.13a-11 at the text (law.cornell.edu). Not read: any built refusal, and the cell or popover in the build.*

**The factual claim in string 1 and string 3 is true: Rule 13a-11(b).** "This section shall not apply to foreign governments, foreign private issuers required to make reports on Form 6-K ... pursuant to 240.13a-16, issuers of American Depositary Receipts ..." So a foreign private issuer is not required to file a Form 8-K, and "whose filers are not required to file 8-Ks" is right. Marketing's own caution is right too: "not required to", not "do not file", because a company may file one voluntarily.

**Required change 1: "the desk cannot check" and "the check ... cannot be run" say more than the rule being built says.** The refusal under option (a) is by the count's form, whatever the company files: a 20-F filer that files an 8-K item 3.02 voluntarily would still be refused. The true statement is about what the desk does, not what it could do. Strings: "Cannot verify: this company's filed share count is from a Form 20-F or 40-F, whose filers are not required to file 8-Ks, so the desk does not check for unregistered share sales since the count." The page sentence: "A company whose filed count is from a Form 20-F or 40-F is not required to file 8-Ks, so for it the check for an unregistered-sale report is not run, and the name reads Cannot verify." The Cannot verify bullet: "the count is from a Form 20-F or 40-F and we do not check for unregistered sales". All three assume Integration builds option (a); if it does not, none of them ships.

**Required change 2: the equity-line sentence states what each sale files, a universal I cannot support.** "Each such sale files nothing unless the running total ... reaches the item 3.02 size" is about every sale to every equity-line investor. I wrote that form in 21c; my own limits in the same read were that I searched three equity-line prospectuses partially, found Section 4(a)(2) in two of them, and measured no frequency. A company can file at a put for its own reasons (a prospectus supplement, a voluntary 8-K), and the sentence's "under an exemption from registration and resells under a registered prospectus" is true of the two I read, not of equity lines in general. A sentence about what the CHECK sees is true by construction: "Neither do sales of newly issued shares to an equity-line investor, who buys on the company's demand: the checks would see one only through a final prospectus or a report of an unregistered sale, and Form 8-K item 3.02 requires such a report only when the running total since the company's last such report or last periodic report reaches the item 3.02 size." It drops the exemption-and-resale clause, which the page does not need, and keeps "requires ... only when" as the rule's own statement (Item 3.02(b)) rather than a claim about what companies file.

**Dependency, and what v21 is not.** v21 still carries the sentences that 21 and 21b require changed ("a final prospectus for an offering of shares", "Resale prospectuses and prospectus updates (such as forms 424B2 and 424B3) do not trigger the check", the cover attribution "(Rule 12b-2)", "since the last report"), and Integration has ruled 424B2 into the check, so the "we look for" list gains 424B2. The next version applies 21, 21b, 21c, 21d and 21e together; it also waits on Research's answer to his cell question (items 1, 2 and 9). I pass nothing on v21 as a whole.

## 22. Float draft v23 (Marketing `ca35884e`, `docs/marketing/float-page-draft.md`): Web's items 1, 2 and 9 and FORM B, fifth tenure, 2026-10-07 (UTC): NOT READY, four required changes; no lower bound or probability appears anywhere, and the cell keeps "≤ X"

*Read: the v23 diff against `2c12b2e4` and the text of the file (no em dash, bytes and entity form, 0); Research's `docs/research/float-contract/bounds-feasibility-2026-10-07.md` (`b3a7ed20`) whole; at `a8f5fd80` `lib/research/insiderBound.ts` (`holdingsAsOf`, `holdingsAtBoth`, `floatCell`); my own 5l and 5o; the raw insider-sale measurement `4454f524` (the summary fields of both datasets in `result.json`). Sources opened for the definition, tonight: Nasdaq's glossary entry "Float" (nasdaq.com/glossary/f/float) and Investopedia's "Floating Stock" (updated 15 December 2023). The Investor.gov glossary pages render with script and gave no text. Not read: Research's written S1 result (I found only the raw), the cell, popover and legend as built, and Design's A and B click-through sketches (`7f2d6267`).*

**Passes.** (a) Item 9: the cell keeps "≤ X". Research is right that no lower bound is defensible from the filings (a complete list of what is held out cannot be built; 13D and 13G totals are beneficial and took one test name below zero; restricted shares appear in no field the job reads), and that "probably not more than" has no measurement behind it (no true float is observed on the desk). v23 states neither, and "Estimated float: 12.3M" is not adopted. (b) The page's "A sale not yet filed by the date the filings were read can still make the float larger than the bound" is the 5l sentence and stays. (c) The lined-up clause "no sale ... filed since the count" and "holdings dated at or before the count" are gone, as 5l and 5o required, and "insider holdings as filed through {fetch date}, counting any sale filed since the count" is the wording 5o passed. (d) Everything from reads 21 to 21f is unchanged in v23.

**Required change 1: the lede's "one common definition ... Another ... is a different number" is not what the sources say, and "the one this page and this desk use" overstates the desk.** Both sources I opened define float as the shares available for trading and give the subtraction as the way to compute it: Nasdaq's glossary entry counts the shares that are outstanding and available for public trading, excluding insiders' and restricted stock; Investopedia's entry calls floating stock the shares available for trading and says it is calculated by subtracting closely held and restricted shares from total outstanding shares, closely held being the shares owned by insiders, major shareholders and employees. So the subtraction is a method for the same quantity, not a second definition, and "affiliates" is the SEC's term, not these sources'. The accurate statement is the expert's point: the computed number is not the same as what can trade on a given day, because insiders can sell. And the desk subtracts only the direct holdings of those who file Forms 3, 4 and 5, not "insiders and affiliates"; it computes an upper bound on the usual figure, not the usual figure. A true form: "Float is the number of a company's shares available for public trading. It is usually computed as shares outstanding less the shares held by insiders and other closely held or restricted shares. This desk computes an upper bound on that number from SEC filings, subtracting only the insider holdings it can see. The shares that can trade on a given day can be more, because insiders can sell." "Trade freely" and "a quoted number" go.

**Required change 2: the claim must carry its definition wherever it can be read alone.** The popover's first sentence "The float is no more than {X} shares when the filings line up", the tooltip's "the true float is at most this", and the face's "≤" are true under the usual computation and false under "available to trade", the reading the expert took. The legend names it ("shares outstanding less insider holdings"); the popover and tooltip do not. Put the definition inside the sentence: "Taking float as shares outstanding less insider holdings, it is no more than {X} shares when the filings line up", and the same in the tooltip. A reader who only opens the popover must not be left with "the number of shares that can trade is at most X".

**Required change 3: the S1 sentence says "reported" where the rule says "dated", leaves "the date shown with the figure" ambiguous, and "is counted" can be read either way.** `holdingsAsOf` selects, per owner and class, the latest row whose OWN date (transaction or period date) is on or before the cutoff, across every filing including one made after the cutoff; `holdingsAtBoth` takes the cutoff at the count's as-of date and at D and subtracts `min(h0, hD)`; an owner with no row at the count's date is not subtracted whatever they filed later. (1) "The last one they reported on or before the count's date" reads as a filing date; say "their holding as of the count's date". (2) The date shown beside the figure is the filed count's date, so "the date shown with the figure" reads as the same date as the first, making the two holdings one. The second cutoff is D, the board's or checkpoint's date: name it with the label the popover already uses ({D-label}). (3) "A sale an insider has filed since the count is counted" does not say which way; say "is added back: the shares sold are no longer subtracted". A true form: "For each insider we take the smaller of two holdings: their holding as of the count's date, and their latest holding dated on or before {D-label}. So a sale an insider has filed since the count is added back, and a sale not yet filed cannot be seen." The popover's "counting any sale filed since the count" has the same corner: a sale dated after D is not counted even if the filing was read (a safe-direction case, the number stays higher); say "counting any sale dated on or before {D-label} that was filed by {fetch date}", or accept the looser wording knowing the corner.

**Required change 4 (Marketing's open question): the "as of {filed-count date}" dating no longer states the claim, and one cell must not make two.** Under S1 the claim holds at D under the stated conditions (5l): the count is at most the stored count at D when nothing was issued, the issuance check runs through D, and S1 subtracts no more than each insider holds at D. So the bound is as of D, not as of the count's date. "As of the count's date" is still true (weaker), but the popover now says "is no more than", present tense, and the legend, the aria-label and the off-popover string would say "as of the date shown ... the filed share count's". Choose one date for the claim and label the other as an input. A true set: aria-label "no more than {X} shares as of {D-label}, computed from the share count dated {filed-count date} and insider holdings filed through {fetch date}, when the filings line up"; the off-popover string the same shorter, "no more than {X} shares as of {D-label}, when the filings line up, from SEC filings" (a stored copy carries the date it holds for); the legend "Float: shares outstanding less insider holdings, computed from SEC filings. An upper bound as of the date of the board or report, when the filings line up. The date beside the number is the filed share count's." The 4-day span is why D, not the fetch date, is the claim's date: a row read more than four days before D reads Cannot verify (`FILINGS_NOT_REREAD`).

**S1's measurement, stated as far as I read it.** The raw `result.json` has two datasets. In both the registered control passes in both directions (a sale subtracts the lower holding; a purchase or grant subtracts the count-date holding). In the first draw (36 names) 5 of 31 subtracted owners have a row filed after the count and none a sale, and S1 changes nothing. In the fresh draw (35 names) 10 of 50 subtracted owners have a later row, 5 owners show a sale, and 61,337 shares that today's rule would subtract are not subtracted under S1. So S1 has met real sales in one draw (5 owners) and the arithmetic of the change is visible there. I did not find Research's written result and read only the raw's summary fields, not the per-name rows. FORM B is not final until that result is read; the wording above does not depend on it.

**What waits.** His A or B click-through choice; the click-through words; the page's final read after these four changes. The "The float is no more than" present tense is acceptable under S1 once the date is stated as D (change 4).

**Required change 1: row 2 defines the number and then calls it an upper bound on itself.** The row reads "Float, as computed here: Shares outstanding less the insider holdings the desk can see. This is an upper bound on that figure, not on the shares that can trade on a given day". The value is exactly what {X} is, so "an upper bound on that figure" bounds {X} by {X}. What is bounded is the float on the usual computation, which subtracts all insider and other closely held shares; {X} subtracts only the insider holdings the desk can see. A true form: "Float, taking it as shares outstanding less insider holdings. The number above subtracts only the insider holdings the desk can see, so it is an upper bound on that figure, not on the shares that can trade on a given day, which can be more, because insiders can sell." (The lede's wording in v25, "an upper bound on that computed figure", has the same shape and passes there because the figure is the one the lede has just defined as the usual computation.)

**Required change 2: row 3's "from a {form} dated {filed-count date}" can read as the form's own date.** The count is the cover value as of a date (`count.asOf`), which is not the form's period date; elsewhere v25 says "the share count dated {filed-count date}". Say "{shares} shares as of {filed-count date}, from a {form} filed {count filed date}." (The payload fields it needs, the count's shares and form, are not there yet, as Marketing says.)

**Required change 3: the per-insider row.** (a) "{owner}: {shares} shares" is a direct holding as reported on Forms 3, 4 and 5; indirect holdings are not subtracted and not shown, so a name beside a number can be read as that person's whole stake. Say "{owner}: {shares} shares held directly". (b) The date and the filed dates must be those of the holding whose shares are shown. The subtracted shares are `min(h0, hD)` for the chosen security title, but after `2add4b2b` `S1Holding.asOf` is still that title's `h0AsOf` (the count-date holding's date) and `filingDates` is the union of the filing dates of both holdings, so a row built from those fields would show the count-date holding's date beside the later, smaller number when a sale was added back. Build condition for Research and Design: the row's "holding dated" is the date of the holding actually subtracted (the later one when a filed sale was added back, the count-date one otherwise), and "filed" lists the filings of that holding. If the payload cannot carry that, the per-insider rows do not ship, as Marketing already says.

**Required change 4: row 5 says "holders who report on Schedule 13D or 13G" are not subtracted, which is false of a holder who also files Form 4.** A 10 percent owner who files Forms 3 and 4 has its direct holding subtracted; what is not subtracted is the beneficial-ownership TOTAL on the 13D or 13G (the page already says "the beneficial-ownership totals in 13D or 13G filings"). Say "the beneficial-ownership totals reported on Schedule 13D or 13G". And "owners with no recent filing" omits the case the code states: `holdingsAtBoth` does not subtract an owner or class with no holding as of the count's date, whatever they filed later. Say "owners with no holding on file as of the count's date or within the filing window". The direction sentence ("Leaving each of them out can only leave this figure higher than the true one, when the filings line up") is true of each item under the headline's definition and under the lede's, so it stands.

**Ruling, owner names on the panel (Marketing asked Commercial and Integration): acceptable, on the signed-in board only, with the two conditions above.** The holdings come from Forms 3, 4 and 5, which are public SEC filings, and the names are those of officers, directors and 10 percent owners of listed companies; I see no licence or terms issue in the desk showing them to signed-in testers. They do not go on the public page, in reports or in exports without a separate read, and "held directly" (change 3a) is what keeps a name from being read as a whole stake. Integration confirms; if it does not want names, the row reads "an insider: {shares} shares held directly, ..." and Marketing rewords.

**Research's written S1 result, now read (`docs/research/float-contract/insider-sale-result-2026-10-07.md` at `72bf286c`), and what it does to 22b's FORM B.** It exists; in 22 and 22b I had found only the raw. It reports the controls passing and S1 raising the bound by exactly the shares sold on 5 real sales (one name, TBPH), never lowering it on 71 names, and changing no printed cell in the sample. It also reports two defects in the UNSAFE direction, both measured by Research and both fixed in the build: a run of same-day rows in one filing kept the FIRST row, the largest after a run of sales, so the bound was too low (fixed by taking the smallest of a (class, date) group within a filing, `def26414`); and one security filed under two titles ("common stock" and "common stock, $0.0001 par value", KITT, 782,829 shares each) was subtracted twice, 10.5 percent of its count (fixed by subtracting only the smallest title's `min(h0, hD)` per owner, `2add4b2b`, of which `def26414` is an ancestor; both are on `research/i-d-424b2`). **FORM B's "no more than {X} shares as of {D-label}" is licensed only with both fixes in the shipped build and the float chain's gate passing, as Research writes; my 22b pass of the wording stands on that condition.** The result does not license "near the number", a rate of insider sales, or that S1 was exercised on a printed cell (it was not, in the sample). Integration confirms the release carries both commits.

**28a. What time the evening note prints (Integration's question; Ops's reading of `lib/research/interpret/pipeline.ts:383`), fifth tenure, 2026-10-07 (UTC): the code at origin says the END of interpretation, which contradicts Ops's "START"; reported, not resolved.** *Read at `origin/main` (`5084cb89`): `lib/research/interpret/pipeline.ts` 245, 278, 340 to 348, 383 and 499, and the three production callers of `interpretTicker` (`worker/research.ts` 668, `worker/research-interpret.ts` 156 and 192).* `generatedAt` is `(options.now ?? new Date()).toISOString()` at line 383, and the awaited model calls are at 245 (`interpreter.interpret`), 278 (`repair`) and 340 to 348 (escalation), all BEFORE it; line 383 follows the validation that produces `accepted`, and the version is saved at 499. No production caller passes `now`, so `options.now` is undefined and the timestamp is the moment the accepted dossier is assembled, after the model has answered, within the cycle computation of the save. The line is unchanged since the interpreter was added (`e889cf12`). So at origin `generated_at` is the completion of interpretation, not its start. **If that reading is right, "Research written again at {time}, after this checkpoint." is true as written:** the version was built at that time, and a version whose job started before the checkpoint and finished after it correctly reads "after". **If Ops's reading is right** (a deployed build that differs from origin, or a path I did not read), the time is the START of the run and the note's "written ... at" is false by the length of the model's latency (a job that finished in 5.6 minutes would print a time up to 5.6 minutes before the version existed), and a version saved after the checkpoint could print a time before it. **Ask:** Ops re-reads lines 245 and 383 and the deployed sha before the evening ship, or the note drops the clock and says "Research written again after this checkpoint." until it does. The condition for printing the note should be a version newer than the one the line was built from, not a timestamp comparison alone, if the two timestamps can disagree.

**34. May real market data appear in screenshots on a public marketing page? (Integration 35th's question (a), for his ask of 2026-10-08T00:30:09.515Z: a full feature accounting, screenshots of every feature, a redone public front page), sixth tenure, 2026-10-08 (UTC): NO for every field that is Alpaca market data, whatever its date, delay or ticker; one tier is unsettled and his; the rest can appear.** *Read, public sources only: Alpaca's Terms and Conditions in full (`files.alpaca.markets/disclosures/library/TermsAndConditions.pdf`, 4 pages, re-downloaded and read 2026-10-08, the Content definition and the personal-use paragraph); the UTP Plan Data Policies published September 2023 (`utpplan.com/DOC/Datapolicies.pdf`, 32 pages, through pymupdf: the Delayed Data Policy, Controlled and Uncontrolled Product definitions, the Trial/Demonstration, News Media and Public Data and Real-Time Volume Only policies); the CTA Consolidated Volume Policy FAQ (March 2015). Earlier, in `docs/commercial/2026-09-07-feed-licence-landscape.md`: sections 1, 1b, the by-field table and 9b. Not read: the NYSE Market Data Complete Policy Package, Nasdaq Basic, Cboe and IEX policies for any screenshot or advertising clause; Alpaca's Customer Agreement beyond the section 30 quoted in the landscape. Nothing here is legal advice; what an exchange would do about one screenshot is not published.*

**1. The governing text is Alpaca's, and it has no date, delay or redaction exception.** "Content is provided exclusively for personal and noncommercial access and use. No part of the Service or Content may be copied, reproduced, republished, uploaded, posted, publicly displayed, encoded, translated, transmitted or distributed in any way (including 'mirroring') to any other computer, server, web site or other medium for publication or distribution or for any commercial enterprise, without Alpaca's express prior written consent." Content is defined to include "(3) market data such as quotations for securities transactions and/or last sale information", "(2) ... information and data concerning the financial markets, securities" and "(7) any other information". The word "historical" does not appear in the document and the only delay language is a liability disclaimer, so a past date and a delayed price are the same Content (the landscape's 9b read, re-confirmed on the text). A marketing page is a web site "for publication ... or for any commercial enterprise".

**2. The exchange-side carve-outs belong to Vendors, and the desk is not one.** UTP: delayed information (a 15 minute interval) on Controlled Products carries no charge but needs a Prominent Delay Message and, for the vendor, the Vendor Agreement; end-of-day information on Uncontrolled Products is not fee liable and needs no Vendor Agreement; "Vendors may publish End-of-Day and Historic Information in print media"; Vendors may show information as an advertisement in a public area open to the public. Each is a permission to a Vendor under its agreement with the plan. The desk holds no such agreement, its data arrives under a personal-use account, and no text read lets a subscriber use a vendor's waiver. Even for a vendor the same policy says "Display of information in the public internet ... does not qualify as a bona fide news story, unless specifically approved", with a duty to contact UTP Plan Administration beforehand. So neither "make it a past date" nor "make it 15 minutes old" converts a screenshot into a licensed one. (The CTA consolidated-volume display statement for advertising applies only where CTA volume sits beside non-CTA prices; every figure here is SIP, so it is not the bar.)

**3. By field.**
- **Cannot appear (Alpaca market data and its single-security price-bearing derivatives):** last price, bid, ask and spread, open, high, low, close, share volume (placed on the cautious side: the exchange layer treats volume-only derived data as non-fee-liable, which is why relative volume sits in the next tier), VWAP, Move % from a reference close, any chart of price, any level taken from bars; and any Alpaca News headline or text (the newswire read: Alpaca News is under the same clause). Not by date, not by delay. A number blurred so it cannot be read is not shown; a number a reader can read is.
- **Unsettled, his call (the desk's own computation from many securities or from volume alone):** relative volume, rank, structure and cycle labels, the Setup grade letters, counts of movers and the breadth line. At the exchange layer UTP calls volume-only and multi-security derived data non-fee-liable and says distribution of it "does not require the Recipient to sign the applicable Agreements"; at Alpaca's layer the clause names "Content", and no text read says whether a number computed from Content is part of it. This is exposure that is arguable, not cleared.
- **Can appear:** the product's layout, labels, legends and hover words; ticker symbols as names; the SEC-sourced float bound with its dated claim and filing dates (public filings, not Alpaca); the report's section headings, summary frame and the desk's own wording; and **sample data** (item 4).

**4. Sample data is the compliant way to show what the data looks like.** A screenshot built on invented numbers is not Content. Two conditions, both claims rules and not licence rules: label it on the image or beside it ("Sample data, not a real day" or equivalent); and use fictional symbols, never a real ticker with an invented price, volume or catalyst, which would be a false statement about a real company and, beside a real-looking mover, an implied record of a call. Real tickers with real moves are also a hindsight selection even where licensed; that belongs to part (b), and no real-day example should be used as proof of the product without it.

**5. His 2026-09-08 ruling does not by its words reach this.** *"accept exposure ... the moment we have something moving, i'll sign up"* settles the first outside viewer, a person, on the desk. A public page is publication to anyone and to search, and the landscape's 1b already names "screenshots" and "a site showing live prices" as the public surface from which discovery follows, "a function of visibility, not of volume". Whether he accepts that exposure for the front page is his to say and no seat extends the ruling. If he does, nothing in this read changes: the screenshots would be an accepted breach of Alpaca's clause, not a licensed use.

**Recommendation to Integration, for the briefs to Marketing and Design:** shoot two sets until he rules. Set one, chrome and structure: layout, filters, drawers, report frames, with every price, volume, quote and Alpaca-news field removed or replaced. Set two, sample data on fictional symbols, labelled. Hold real-data screenshots for a display licence or his explicit acceptance, and carry the unsettled tier (relative volume, rank, grades, mover counts) in sample data too rather than decide it by default. Each screenshot's caption and the page's claims come to this seat as part (b).

Not read: Design's case list, any build of cases 2 and 3, the renders.* Why the new cases exist, from the code: `narrativeChanged` means the narrative was re-written; `revisionLine` then says "the summary was written for this edition" (and, with no names changed, "Revised ...: the summary was written for this edition; every line is carried"). If the re-write comes back empty the report's `narrative` is null and the card region prints "No summary in this report.", so the built line says a summary was written beside a card saying there is none. The strings are the right repair and they say what the report holds, never why. Checked: Case 2 "Published again at {10:02 pm} ET; every line is carried from the {8:30 pm} edition. Every line is carried from the {8:30 pm} edition.": "Revised" is true because the report differs from the earlier edition by exactly that, and it states the loss without a cause. The names-written variants add "This edition has no summary, and the {8:30 pm} edition had one." or "This edition has no summary.": true under the same conditions. **Required condition.** The payload's `revisionOf` carries only the earlier edition's time, `changedTickers` and `narrativeChanged`; it says nothing about whether the 8:30 edition HAD a summary. "Neither edition has a summary" and "the 8:30 edition had one" are claims about that other edition, so the renderer may print them only from the earlier edition's own stored narrative, read by the same test the card region uses to print "No summary in this report." for this one (one predicate, two editions, not a second definition of "has a summary"). Where the earlier edition is not available to the page, or its narrative cannot be read, print only what is true of this edition: "Published again at {10:02 pm} ET; every line is carried from the {8:30 pm} edition. This edition has no summary." for case 2, and for case 3 the same sentence ("Revised" is not claimed without the earlier edition). Design confirms the source before it builds; the built strings come back here. Advisory: when this edition has no summary and `narrativeChanged` is false, the old section 1 line "every line and the summary are carried" is false the same way, so the no-summary test must run before the choice among the old strings, not only inside the re-written branch.

For the EARLIER edition only NARRATIVE ("had one") and NONE ("has none") may license the earlier-edition clauses; V5_OVERALL or null means the page does not know, and the fallback applies, because a pre-v6 report can hold an `overall` opening and "Neither edition has a summary" would be false of it. Built strings for each case come back here.

Ops's reader opened (`lib/market/setupGradesRead.ts` at `f531e1af`, lines 1 to 30 and 88 to 128; Design had read it and said so, I read it myself), seventh tenure, 2026-10-09 (UTC): the absent-name advisory is answered, and a premise of reads 42 to 47 is narrower than the code.** Absent: a name is left out of the map only when NO COMPLETE checkpoint holds a row for it (`ABSENT`); incomplete checkpoints are never read. So "The desk has no research on this name at this checkpoint" is true of the checkpoint shown, as Design said, and the advisory in read 47 is withdrawn. **Null is broader than "predates grading".** The header comment says a null symbol is one whose latest COMPLETE row predates v29, but the code sets null whenever the latest row's grade is null, OR not in the `GRADES` set, OR its basis does not parse, OR its rule or `completedAt` is missing. A row that carries a stored grade the reader cannot use is shown with the null words, "The desk recorded no setup grade at this checkpoint", and the legend says "no grade was recorded for the name"; both are false of that row, because a grade WAS recorded and the desk could not show it. Reached when a rubric change adds a stored value before the `GRADES` set learns it, or when a basis is malformed. It is not reached by the expected data (a pre-v29 row), which is why none of reads 42 to 47 found it: each took Design's and Marketing's account of the null ("a checkpoint that predates grading") as the whole of it, and I had said I had not opened the reader. **Advisory, not required, and Integration's call:** either accept it as rare and unreached, or word the null for every cause, e.g. hover "No setup grade is shown for this name at this checkpoint." (the legend and chip hover change with it), or make the reader return a separate state for an unreadable stored grade so the null words stay true. Whichever is chosen, a rubric change that adds a stored grade value must update the reader's `GRADES` set in the same change, or every such name prints a dash.

''}`, which is where the raw ISO timestamp in the drawer comes from, and the exhibit inherits its parent's `publishedAt`, so the date is not lost); `computeCatalystFingerprint` in `lib/research/fingerprint.ts` (url, content hash, `publishedAt`, cluster; the title is not an input, so a new template cannot move a fingerprint). **51. The five source-label answers (Marketing `8750f61d`, `source-title-words-2026-10-09.md`, to Design's rules draft `docs/Design/source-labels-draft-2026-10-09.md` at `68c6ebc1`), seventh tenure, 2026-10-09 (UTC): PASS, with one required line.** *Read: both documents whole. Not read: the drawer code or EDGAR's description list (Marketing says it did not read the list either); not run.* (1) Dropping a description that only restates the form, and keeping any other in the filing's own words: a kept description is attributed to the filer and the label asserts nothing about what the filing means, so no claim is made that the document does not make about itself. The rule as written is a semantic test; the code cannot run one, so Design implements it as a closed list in data (the seven names, and a description equal to the form, compared without case) with everything else kept. A miss then costs a redundant label, never a false one. (2) "{date}, {exhibitType} to {parentForm}" agrees with read 50, so stored and rebuilt rows read the same. (3) "(2)" for a second different filing whose label is identical, no mark on the first, one entry for identical URLs: it never tells a reader there is one filing where there are two, which is the claim at risk. (4) "; " after " · sources ": presentation. (5) The year when it is not the reader's ET year: right, and it is what keeps a bare "Oct 5" from being the unanchored clock of the revision lines. **Required, one line:** the label's date is the ET date of `publishedAt` (the board's rule and Marketing's year rule are both ET; the draft says only "from `publishedAt`"). Not run: the tests (Research reports `floatJob.test.ts` and the six mutants with nine kills).* The condition (the reason is stored only on a 404): `computeFloatBound` wraps only the companyfacts fetch in a try; the catch swallows an error only when it is a `SecRequestError` with `status === 404`, and rethrows every other error, so no row is stored for anything else. In the client a non-retryable status throws with that status (the 404 case), a retryable status (403, 429, 5xx) is retried and then thrown with its own status, a network failure carries no 404, and unparseable JSON carries the response's status (200); none of them can reach the catch as a 404. The submissions request is outside the try. `floatCell` returns `UNKNOWN / COMPANY_FACTS_NOT_AVAILABLE` after the NO_CIK test and before the split and no-count tests, so a no-CIK name keeps its own reason. The string in `UNKNOWN_TEXT` is Marketing's `fd7b7322` word for word, face Unknown. The wire reader passes any string `reason` through and the display looks it up in `UNKNOWN_TEXT`, so the new reason prints its own sentence and not the "No filed share count." fallback; there is no CHECK constraint on the stored reason (`db.ts` 350 is a comment naming the count kinds, now one short, harmless). **One correction to my own condition.** Read 54 said an outage must read "Cannot verify". The job's documented rule (header of `floatJob.ts`) is that a throwing fetch aborts the call, no row is stored, and the cell stays "Not computed"; that is unchanged, it is true ("The desk has not computed a float for this name."), and it satisfies what the condition was for, that the popover never says SEC's data "returned nothing" when SEC was down. I had named the wrong display for the fault path. The page's Unknown line (`666b989a`) ships with this release; nothing else is owed on it from this seat.

**61. The live "priced $1 to $20" and the selector's default (Marketing's question from Integration 37th; code at `aaa29c91` for the server, `5e8b13c3` for the control), seventh tenure, 2026-10-09 (UTC): the live sentence is false of today's board, and shipping the selector does not by itself make it true.** *Read: `lib/market/readModel.ts` 607 to 625, `app/api/market/route.ts` 17 to 30, `lib/market/config.ts` 50 to 82 and 131 to 138 at `aaa29c91`; `components/PriceRangeControl.tsx` 20 to 80 and the shell's range handling at `5e8b13c3`. Taken from Integration and Ops, not read by me: that the worker's band floor was $0.25 this morning.* What the code does. With no range in the request, `readModel` serves the engine's own band (`served = options.range ? resolveRequestedRange(...) : band`, `band` from the worker's stored summary), and the route says "Absent = the engine's own band". The $1 to $20 default (`boardDefaultMin` and `boardDefaultMax`, whose comment reads "The range the board opens on. His ruling: $1-$20") is used only inside `resolveRequestedRange`, that is, when a range IS asked for. The control tells itself otherwise: its `onChange(null)` is documented "returns to the default", the shell sends nothing for null, and `isDefault` compares the served range with `rangeDefault`. So a viewer who never touches the control is served the worker's band, not $1 to $20; the control shows the non-default highlight and a "reset to $1–$20" button, and pressing it sends nothing and changes nothing. If the worker's band is $0.25 to something, the live front page ("priced $1 to $20", section 3 and the ledger row), the planned "unless you change the range" and the control's "reset to $1–$20" are all untrue of what an untouched viewer sees. **Ruling, mine, for him and Integration.** The selector is not the fix unless the box test shows it. The test: open the board as a viewer who has never touched the control (no stored range) and read the header caption; it must read "$1–$20". If it does, the front page's sentence is true again and "unless you change the range" (read 60) goes with the selector. If it reads anything else, the cure is in the code (the server applies `rangeDefault` when no range is asked) or the worker's band, not in a sentence; and until that is true the front page must not state a range. **Fallback sentence, true of any band, for section 3 and its ledger row:** "The board ranks the common stocks and ADRs the desk tracks, in the price range shown at the top of the board, by how much each has traded against its own twenty-session norm." (the header caption prints the served range, `discovery.priceMin` and `priceMax`, so it is true by construction). The registered plain statement ("built for people who trade $1–$20 small-cap momentum") is about who the desk is built for and stays. The change of a live sentence needs his yes through Integration; if the box test fails tonight, ask him for the fallback in the same message.

The sentence describes the feature when it is warm and is not made false by a state the screen names; I do not ask for a qualifier. **Condition 2, the kill switch.** `config.boardRangeOff` serves the band board and hides the control. The sentence describes the feature, not the failure. **Advisory, no change required:** the mark has no word; a hover on it ("Ranking your range" or the like) would say what it means and would be a new string for this seat; the bounded fallback was accepted by Integration at `e8f82643` and I do not ask to reopen it under the freeze.

In Research's frame an entry is UNKNOWN for three reasons (`morningRead.ts` 172 to 177): `GRADE_NOT_STORED` (a stored grade is null on either side), `NOT_GRADED` (either side's grade is the string UNKNOWN or NOT_YET), `VERSION_UNREADABLE` (a research version on either side cannot be read), and each returns UNKNOWN BEFORE any part is compared. So under every UNKNOWN the comparison did not happen for that name: both checkpoints exist and were read, but "Compared: {evening} and {morning}" asserts a comparison of them, which for this name was not made. The line is untrue there, the same defect the AIXC fix removed where it contradicted NOT_YET's detail. **Required:** for state UNKNOWN set `compared` to null in every branch (the two branches that already lack a time print none: no evening recap, and the evening time missing); the face "Unknown" stands alone, as it does for an unreadable read. No new words. For the states that did compare (UNCHANGED, REVISED, NOT_REREAD) the line is true and stays; for NOT_YET and the row-5 "Evening recap: {evening ET}." the existing rulings stand.
