**72. The theme tag's closed list and what a sentence built on it can say (Research plan `9b257525`, `docs/research/theme-assessment/plan-c-2026-10-11.md`; his yes to build the theme line, Integration chat 2026-10-11T20:04:41.211Z; Integration's three questions), seventh tenure, 2026-10-11 (UTC): the plan may proceed on FOUR CONDITIONS; the list as written is not assignable truthfully from one catalyst without them.** *Read: the plan whole; the catalyst block of `lib/research/interpret/schema.ts` (`catalystStatus` FRESH_FOUND, SECONDARY_STALE, NONE_FOUND, CONFLICTING, UNKNOWN; `catalystType` incl. NONE_IDENTIFIED) and the `CATALYST_NONE_REQUIRES_COVERAGE` guardrail in `guardrails.ts` 410 to 428 at `9b257525`. Not read: the prompt text, which does not exist yet.* **Q1, are the values distinct enough?** No, not for a single value picked by a model. AI overlaps BIOTECH_MEDICAL (AI drug discovery), CRYPTO (mining sold as AI compute), ENERGY_NUCLEAR (reactors for data centers) and DEFENSE_DRONES; QUANTUM overlaps DEFENSE_DRONES. Each overlap is a case where two honest taggers pick differently, so a count of a value is a count of one model's pick. **Condition 1:** the instruction must say the tag is the SUBJECT OF THE EVENT the cited source reports (what the company announced, signed, acquired or launched), not a word that appears in it; when two list items fit, the one the event is first about; an event that only mentions a theme, or mentions it in passing in a financing or a corporate release, is OTHER. That answers Integration's example: a press release that only mentions AI is not tagged AI. **Q2, is NO_CATALYST the same as NONE_FOUND?** Only if it is derived from it. `NONE_FOUND` is guarded: when source coverage was incomplete the guardrail downgrades it to UNKNOWN, because "none found" asserts an adequate search. A theme value the model picks has no such guard, so a name whose status the guardrail refused could still carry NO_CATALYST, and a report counting NO_CATALYST would say "none found" of names whose search was incomplete. **Condition 2:** NO_CATALYST is set by code from `catalystStatus === 'NONE_FOUND'` after the guardrail, never by the model; a theme is a model pick only when the status is FRESH_FOUND or SECONDARY_STALE (a catalyst was identified); for CONFLICTING, UNKNOWN and a name not researched the field is null ("not tagged"), counted as neither OTHER nor NO_CATALYST, which the plan already says for pre-schema-4 versions. The plan's separate "no news found" axis is then the same fact as NO_CATALYST and must not be counted twice. **Q3, what could a public or report sentence not say truly?** (a) That the names are "AI stocks" or "AI companies": the tag is of the catalyst's subject, not the company's business (the industry axis is the SIC description; the country axis "based in", read earlier). (b) Any causal word, "driven by", "on", "because of" the theme: the tag says what the news was about, and read 29 and 36 rule that no sentence connects a move to its news. (c) "Winners": the plan's G is the 12 names with the largest stored percent change on any completed checkpoint of that date, in the report's range; that is a move at a checkpoint, not a finish, and a name can have faded; say "the 12 names with the largest move on the desk's checkpoints" or the like, never "winners" or "the day's biggest". (d) A topic count without its denominator: because a name with an unchanged dossier is never retagged (`pipeline.ts` 204 to 208, the plan's own reuse gap), the tagged winners are the names that had new evidence; **Condition 3:** every topic count prints how many of the window's names were tagged ("3 of the 9 tagged"), and topic stays silent until the plan's 20 windows of tagged history exist, as the plan says. (e) "Theme" as a market claim: the line may say a count is above its usual level over a stated window; it may not say money, interest or momentum is flowing into the subject. **Condition 4:** the words, which come here through Marketing, state the window (two trading days), the base rate comparison and the tag source ("the desk's research tagged"). Not asked and not a condition: the list is his to name (the plan says so); a new value later is a schema bump; the "based in" country wording and the SIC map are read when worded. I did not run anything.

**73. The rebuilt topic tag, ahead of the build (Research sizing `cd4b2b0e`, `docs/research/theme-assessment/list-review-size-2026-10-12.md`; his review, "yes to both", Integration chat 2026-10-12T17:19:00.133Z; Integration's three questions), seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC): PROCEED on conditions; read 72's conditions 1 and 2 stay binding.** *Read: the sizing whole; read 72's sources (the catalyst schema, `CATALYST_NONE_REQUIRES_COVERAGE`). Not read: his "Covers" text (his paste in Integration's chat, not in the repository), the prompt, the tie rules; the model-facing definitions come here when written.* **(1) Can the 15 topics and ten event types be assigned truthfully from one cited source?** Yes, with two limits the prompt and the code must carry. The array form removes the overlap read 72 found (AI with chips or biotech, defense with drones): a name may carry several topics, so no honest pick is forced. What remains is the line between a substantive topic and a passing mention, which Research itself rates a judgement that can differ between runs on the same news. **Condition A1:** a topic is substantive only when the cited source's event IS about it (a product, contract, partnership, financing, trial or transaction in that topic); a topic the source names in passing goes to the passing array; and nothing may be inferred from what the company usually does or from knowledge outside the source (a drone maker's reverse split carries no DRONES tag). **Condition A2:** the tags are stored against the catalyst's cited source ids, and code empties both arrays and the event-derived fields unless `catalystStatus` is FRESH_FOUND or SECONDARY_STALE and a source is cited. The ten event types are assignable from one source where the event is stated; the government-contract case needs the tie rule ("the event is first about"), which comes here when written. **(2) Multi-label counting, and what a summary sentence may say.** Counting a name once toward each of its substantive topics, never summing, with "k of N tagged", is true; the sentence may say: in a stated window, k of the N tagged names among the 12 with the largest move on the desk's checkpoints had news the desk's research tagged {topic}, that a name can count toward more than one topic, and that M of the window's names carry no tag; that k is above the topic's usual level over a stated history. It may not add topics together ("X of 12 were AI or chips"), rank topics by share, use "most" or "many" without the number, call passing mentions, or say the names are stocks in the topic (read 72). **Condition B:** N is the tagged names and the line says how many were not tagged. **Condition C, fifteen chances a day:** each topic must beat its own 90th percentile over 20 windows, so about one in ten topics clears the bar by chance; with 15 topics a day with something to report is the normal day, and "unusual", "surge", "theme" in the market sense would be untrue of a threshold designed to be crossed. The line must say how many topics were checked ("1 of 15 topics checked is above its usual level") or the bar must be raised until a crossing is rare; either makes the sentence true. **(3) Does "updated" deal status need a guard like NONE_FOUND's?** Yes, and the other three values need a smaller one. `NONE_FOUND` is guarded because it asserts an absence only an adequate search supports; UPDATED asserts that an earlier deal exists and this source revises it, which the model can only know if the source says so or the desk holds the earlier deal. **Condition D:** UPDATED is allowed only when the cited source itself says it amends, extends, revises or supplements an earlier agreement, or is assigned by code when an earlier stored version of this name's catalyst carries the same deal; otherwise the model chooses among the other values or null, and a re-run headline of an old deal is not UPDATED, it is a freshness question the event timestamp already answers. SIGNED and COMPLETED need the source's own words (an agreement entered, a closing completed), not a plan to; PROPOSED is the safe default for an announced intent. A non-null deal status requires a deal-type event (commercial agreement, partnership, M&A or strategic transaction, and a financing agreement if Research counts it one) and is null for every other event type. **First seen in the desk's sources** (6b) is true as worded: the earliest `published_at` among the catalyst's cited sources, the desk's first sighting and not the first public one; keep it off any sentence that says when news broke. **Variability.** A name's tags come from its latest research version before the checkpoint and can change between versions; every sentence says "the desk's research tagged". Cost and hours are Research's. I ran nothing.

**74. The written tag text (Research `b3d1ec43`, `lib/research/interpret/catalystTheme.ts` and the tag block of `guardrails.ts` 430 to 490; Integration's two asks), seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC): PASS, one required change; where it can be shorter.** *Read: `catalystTheme.ts` whole (155 lines: `CATALYST_THEMES`, `CATALYST_EVENTS`, the tie rule, `DEAL_EVENTS`, the four `*_DESCRIPTION` strings, `catalystThemePromptBlock`, `catalystTagsOf`) and the guardrail block. Not run; the measured +1,330 input tokens is Research's.* **Truth of the definitions.** The prompt carries read 72's rule and read 73's A1 verbatim in substance: the tags describe the EVENT the cited source reports, "not a word that appears in the source", "never infer a topic from what the company usually does, from its industry, or from anything outside the cited source". The 15 topics are scoped by what each covers; the examples overlap on purpose (uranium mining under minerals and nuclear fuel under NUCLEAR; storage under ENERGY and batteries under EV_BATTERIES; DEFENSE and DRONES) and the array lets a name carry both when the source's event is about both; AI's "deals the source names as AI" ties it to the source's own words. The ten events are assignable from one source where the event is stated, and the tie rule is the one read 73 asked for ("first about"; a government contract or award is GOVERNMENT_POLICY, a company's COMMERCIAL_AGREEMENT, working together with no purchase PARTNERSHIP). The code meets A2 and D: the guardrail sets NO_CATALYST from the final NONE_FOUND, empties the arrays and the event unless the status is FRESH_FOUND or SECONDARY_STALE with a cited source, de-duplicates and removes any passing topic that is also substantive, and nulls a deal status on an event outside `DEAL_EVENTS`. UPDATED is allowed only on the source's own words to amend, extend or revise, and an old deal headlined again is NOT UPDATED. **Required.** The model's word for the deal's absence is `NOT_A_DEAL`, which the guardrail stores as null, and the prompt gives it for "any other event" and "otherwise". A deal event whose status is none of the four (a terminated, withdrawn or cancelled deal) would therefore be marked NOT_A_DEAL, which is untrue of it. Words only, no new value: describe the none-value as "not a deal, or the source states no status from this list (for example a terminated deal)", in the prompt and in `CATALYST_DEAL_STATUS_DESCRIPTION`, and read null as "no status" everywhere it is counted. **Advisories.** "OTHER_MATERIAL" and "any material event": "material" is a legal conclusion the desk does not draw; keep it internal and let the reader's word be "other". COMPLETED's "or was delivered" should read "or was fulfilled" (delivered of what is unsaid). SIGNED's "a definitive agreement" fits a contract with no closing less well than a deal with one; "signed and not yet completed" is the exact pair with COMPLETED. The theme examples are examples, not a closed list; "covers" is the right word, keep it. **Where it can be shorter without losing a condition.** By count from the file: the 25 "covers" lines are about 2,100 characters of the roughly 5,300 added; the ten whose id says it already (AI, CRYPTO, QUANTUM, NUCLEAR, DRONES, SPACE_SATELLITES, EV_BATTERIES, FINANCIAL_RESULTS_OUTLOOK, REGULATORY_CLINICAL, M_AND_A_STRATEGIC) are about 630 of them, about 160 tokens; the "empty when catalystStatus is not FRESH_FOUND or SECONDARY_STALE" clause is stated in the prompt and again in three schema descriptions (about 330 characters, 80 tokens), and the code already enforces it, so keep it in the prompt once. Together about 240 tokens, under a fifth of the +1,330; the rest is the enum ids carried in both schema and prompt and the rules read 72 and 73 require, which should not be cut. Keep every `covers` that decides a boundary (SEMICONDUCTORS_COMPUTING, ROBOTICS_AUTOMATION, CRITICAL_MINERALS_METALS, FINTECH_PAYMENTS, CONSUMER_RETAIL, DEFENSE, ENERGY "not nuclear", BIOTECH_MEDICAL, and the events COMMERCIAL_AGREEMENT, PARTNERSHIP, FINANCING_CAPITAL, GOVERNMENT_POLICY, CORPORATE_LISTING, OTHER_MATERIAL, PRODUCT_TECH_MILESTONE). The bigger lever is Research's own: whether the block rides the cached prefix.

**75. The report checker's advice-word rule, so a company's own deal facts survive (his yes via Integration, 2026-10-12; Research's test `8f6c9282`, raw `656f461b`; `lib/desk/reports/check.ts` `FORBIDDEN` on `origin/production`, the buy/sell rule at 128, enter/entry at 129, "prediction or certainty" at 135), seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC): the ALLOWED SHAPES.** *Read: the `FORBIDDEN` table and its comments; the voice rulings that put the words there (`docs/research/session-reports/marketing.md` the "words the voice NEVER uses" table, and run 3's "no buy/sell even descriptively"); the standing disclaimer rulings (`commercial.md` sections 2 and 4). Not read: the 12 struck production lines (Integration read them) and Research's struck-deal-fact set; both should come here once for a check that each line is allowed by exactly one shape below. Nothing run.* **The principle.** The ban exists so no line tells a reader what to do ("No buy/sell language, ever. These lines describe observed state, not what to do about it") or promises what happens next. A company's own past or stated action, from a cited filing or release, is observed state. So the verb is allowed only when the COMPANY (or a named counterparty) is the agent, the object is a transaction, and the line carries its citation; in every other position the word stays struck. The regex should REMOVE each allowed shape from the text and then test the remainder, so a line that carries an allowed shape and a banned use is still struck. **S1, "agreed to".** `agreed to (sell|buy|purchase|acquire|issue)`, also `has agreed to` and `agrees to`, when not followed by `into` or `the (news|rip|pop|spike)`. Allowed: "NFT Ltd agreed to sell its Canadian oil and gas business". **S2, the agreement as a noun.** `(agreement|contract|deal|offer|option) to (sell|buy)`. Allowed: "an agreement to sell 80,000,000 shares". **S3, stated intent, attributed.** `(plans|intends|proposes|seeks|offered) to (sell|buy)`; "may", "could", "expected to" are not in it (speculation), and `will` stays banned by its own rule. **S4, a term of art.** `selling (stockholder|stockholders|shareholder|shareholders|securityholders|security holders)`, the registration form's own words for a resale; the bare `selling` rule and its "not proof of selling" exemption are unchanged. **S5, entering an agreement.** `(entered|enters|entering) (into )?(a|an|the|its|their) <up to six words> (agreement|contract|facility|arrangement|lease|licence|license|merger|purchase|sale|offering|plan|term sheet|letter of intent)` and `entry into (a|an|the) <up to six words> agreement` (the filing item's own title). **S6, "confirmed" as a reporting verb.** `(the company|it|management|<a named party of one to four capitalised words>) confirmed that` and nothing else: the subject is the company or a named party, the object is a clause, so it attributes a statement and states no certainty of ours. **What stays struck, and the negative tests to keep.** Buy or sell as a reader-directed or trading act: "sell into strength", "sell the news", "buy the dip", "worth buying", "buyers" and "sellers" as a read, "buying" and "selling" pressure, "agreed to sell into strength". Entry and exit as a trade: "entry" and "entry point", "entered at", "entered the top 12" (also ruled absent by `marketing.md`, `ENTERED_TOP_N`), "enter a position", "exit". "Confirmed" in the desk's own voice or in the passive: "confirmed the breakout", "confirmed by volume", "the catalyst is confirmed", "was confirmed", "confirming", "confirmed the move"; the Morning check prints no "confirmed" for the same reason. `will`, `pop`, `clearly` and `guaranteed` are untouched. Already allowed with no change: "sold N shares in an offering", "bought back", "buyback", "sale", "offering", "divest" (none matches the ban). **Condition: the citation.** The relaxed shapes count only on a line that carries its source citation token (v6 already checks every line for one); an uncited deal fact stays struck, which keeps "every claim must trace to a stored field". **Condition: tests.** Research's struck-deal-fact set must pass, and the advice set above (each example) must still strike; add the pair "agreed to sell ... so sell into strength" (struck by the second use) and "agreed to sell into strength" (struck by S1's exclusion). **Rule on "confirmed":** a reporting verb with the company or a named party as subject and "that" after it is attribution and allowed; in any other use it is the certainty claim the voice table bans ("unless a stored field says so") and stays struck. A re-run of the seeded pass bar is Research's and Ops's; I did not run it.

**75a. Read 75's shapes tested against the real struck lines (Research raw `656f461b`: 48 run files under `docs/research/claude-credits/reports-test-2026-10-12/raw/`; script `docs/commercial/2026-10-12-deal-fact-shapes-check.py`), seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC): the shapes as first written would have left nine of the 46 lines struck; the refined shapes pass all 46 and still strike 16 of 16 advice sentences.** The raw keeps each draft's text and each strike's line id, so the struck text does exist there (Integration was right that production's diagnostics do not keep it). I extracted the 46 distinct draft lines that carry buy, sell, enter or confirmed and were struck as FORBIDDEN_WORDING, and ran the shapes in Python (the ban regexes copied from `check.ts`, the "proof of", "confirmed" and "not" lookbehinds split to fixed width). **What the real lines needed beyond read 75:** (1) a cue word may sit up to about 60 characters before "to sell" or "to buy" without a full stop or semicolon between ("agreed on October 5, 2026 to sell", "a definitive agreement, dated September 30, to sell", "the right to direct Hudson Global Ventures to buy"), and the cue list gains the plurals and "right", "obligation", "allowing", "enabling", "permitting", "direct"; (2) "entered" takes plural, determiner-less objects ("entered agreements to acquire") and "entered a definitive agreement"; the noun list gains "indication of interest", "memorandum", "settlement", "loan", "placement", "subscription", "partnership", "joint venture", and "equipment lease" is covered by "lease"; (3) "agreement, entered October 5, to sell" (a participle after the noun); (4) "confirmed" takes "confirmed that" and "confirmed it had" or "has" ("Nasdaq confirmed it had regained compliance"), and the named party must not be a pronoun or "The stock", "The name", "The move", "Price" (otherwise "It confirmed it had broken out" passes); (5) order: the enter shapes replace their match with a neutral cue (" agreement ") so the sell shape still sees it, otherwise "entered an agreement to sell" loses its cue and strikes. All 46 real lines were deal facts; none was an advice use, so the set has no negative class and the 16 advice sentences are mine ("sell into strength", "worth buying on the dip", "entry at 4.20", "entered the top 12", "enter a position", "confirmed the breakout", "the catalyst is confirmed", "agreed to sell into strength", "agreed to sell shares, so sell into strength", and two compound lines): all 16 stay struck. **Not claimed:** that these shapes are complete for lines no writer has produced yet; that they are JavaScript-equivalent (the lookbehinds and the case-sensitive named-party pattern need the same tests in the checker's own language); the citation condition of read 75 is not testable from this raw. Research ports and re-runs.

**75b. The citation condition of read 75, substituted (Integration's finding; build base `00107b01`), and a correction, seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC).** Opened `lib/desk/reports/check.ts` 440 to 470 at `00107b01`: in a v6 report "a citation token in any line is struck" (`CITATION_TOKEN`, `withoutCitations(line.text) !== line.text`), so no line can carry a source token and read 75's condition ("a line that carries its source citation token") could never be met; Integration is right. `ReportResearch` (`lib/desk/reports/research.ts`) holds, per ticker as of the checkpoint, `catalyst.sources` (the catalyst's cited sources only: "a source the catalyst did not cite" is refused by the shape) and `structure.supply[]`, each block with the filing it rests on. **Substitute, confirmed with one widening:** the deal shapes count only on a line whose ticker has research at the checkpoint that carries at least one cited catalyst source OR at least one supply block that names its filing (a financing fact such as a private placement rests on the supply block, not the catalyst); a line with no ticker (the narrative, a headline) keeps the deal shapes struck; and the model checker's UNSUPPORTED still reads each line against that research, which is the actual trace. Alternative (b), dropping the condition, is not acceptable: it would let a deal fact through on a name with no cited evidence at all. **Correction to my own text.** Read 75's S1 example, "NFT Ltd agreed to sell its Canadian oil and gas business", joined two different companies' lines from Integration's message: the share sale is NFT Ltd's (80,000,000 Class A ordinary shares) and the Canadian oil and natural gas business is Barnwell's (read 75a's run of the raw shows both). An example in this file must not attribute one company's action to another; the shapes are unaffected. The corrected examples are "NFT Ltd entered an agreement to sell 80,000,000 Class A ordinary shares" and "Barnwell agreed to sell its Canadian oil and natural gas business".

**75c. Integration's narrowing of the cue "plan", and a narrower cue list (Research's finding on `reportTraced.test.ts` 183, voice run v5: "A plan to sell shares." on SANG), seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC): AGREED, AND NARROWER STILL.** Research is right: my first cue list admitted the bare noun "plan" with no agent, so an agentless intention to sell, which contradicts "capacity, not proof of selling" on a registered-resale name, passed. I reran `docs/commercial/2026-10-12-deal-fact-shapes-check.py` on the 46 real struck lines of raw `656f461b`, removing each cue in turn: only three are used by any real line, `agree/agrees/agreed` (4 lines need it), `agreement(s)` (20) and `direct` (1); removing contract(s), deal(s), offer(s), option(s), right, obligation, obligated, plan(s), intend(s), propose(s), seek(s), offered, allowing, enabling and permitting strikes nothing that is real. Seven of eight agentless intent phrases I tried passed wrongly under the first list ("A plan to sell shares.", "An offer to sell shares.", "An option to sell shares.", "The right to sell shares.", "An obligation to sell shares.", "Plans to sell shares were filed.", "A proposal seeks to sell shares."). **Narrowed list:** `agree/agrees/agreed`, `agreement(s)`, and `direct <Named party of one to four capitalised words>` (the real "direct Hudson Global Ventures to buy" lines; a bare "direct" would admit "Analysts direct investors to buy"). Drop every intent cue (plans, intends, proposes, seeks, offered) and the possession nouns until a real line needs one, and then only with a company or named party as agent. Result in the committed script: 0 of 46 real lines struck; 0 of 22 advice and agentless sentences pass. One residual I could construct, "Traders agreed to sell the stock at the open.", is the cue verb with a non-company agent; the model checker's UNSUPPORTED read, not a regex, is what catches it, and no writer has produced it. I agree with Integration's ruling; this is a stricter version of it.

**76. Marketing's three hand-written sample session reports, read for truth (`fedbb374`, `docs/marketing/sample-reports-2026-10-12.md`, 143 lines; his yes via Integration 38th; sources the opus-5-5 files of raw `656f461b` for PREMARKET 2026-10-05, AFTER_HOURS 2026-10-06, INTO_POWER_HOUR 2026-10-05), seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC): the figures and the facts hold; four corrections; three notes for Integration.** *Opened: the file whole and the three raw JSONs; by program, for all 36 name lines, every percentage against the card's price path (`from`, `high`, `now`, `fromBasis`) and every other number in a line against its card's text, headline, company and risk row; the three narratives and footnote counts; the checkpoint ids and times; by hand HSCS, MI, XHG and JONE (risk row and freshness), and JENA, MI, OLOX, GLXG and POLA phrases. Not run: the checker or the writer.* **What holds.** Every percentage matches the price path (HSCS down 16 and high 49, OLOX up 84 and 89, MI power-hour up 806 and 1,066, and so on, 36 of 36), and every number that is not a computed percentage is on its card (the script found no number missing). The Summaries are the narratives' own words and counts (9 ran at least 50 percent, 5 kept half, OLOX 84, 2 of 12 fresh in report 1; XHG, IPDN, OLOX, 8 of 12 in report 2; MI, QTEX, ALEC, 2 of 12 in report 3). The report 1 footnote "2 names had volume but no recorded regular-session close" is the stored `noClose` 2. The checkpoints agree with the headings (report 1 is the NEXT_DAY_RECAP completed 2026-10-06T02:00Z, which is 22:00 ET on 10-05; report 2 the RTH_CLOSE completed 2026-10-06T20:00Z, 16:00 ET). **(1) "found in our sources".** It is used where a claim could be read as about the market, and the section headings are the passed ones. Two lines say it without the qualifier: OLN, "The latest company news is Olin and Huntsman shareholders approving ...", and OLOX in report 2, "the latest company update is the September 17 Bitcoin production report" (inside "No catalyst found in our sources:", but "latest" is still a claim about the world). Make them "The latest company news we found is" and "the latest company update we found is". **(2) "After the close" for PREMARKET.** Right: every PREMARKET card has `fromBasis` TODAY_CLOSE, so `from` is the 10-05 close and `now` is after it; the narrative says "After the close, OLOX added 84 percent" and the card computes +84.1 percent; HSCS, POLA, XCH, QTEX and the others compute the same way. **(3) The three supply paraphrases are restatements, not readings.** HSCS: the card's risk detail is "Reported shares outstanding were 4,800,172 as of September 11, 2026", verbatim. MI: the card (DIRTY FLOAT) says the latest affirmative shares-outstanding figure "is dated June 30 and predates both the August offering and the September private-placement agreement"; report 3's "before the August offering and this placement" is complete, and report 1's "before it" is the placement only, true and shorter. XHG: the card says the latest explicitly reported shares-outstanding datapoint "in the bundle" is dated September 30, 2022 and predates the January 2025 100,000-for-1 consolidation; "the latest share count we have is dated 2022" says it. **Corrections.** (a) VNME in report 3 is "Down 1 percent" and its path is -0.6 percent; the file's own rule is "less than 1" under one percent, so it should read "Down less than 1 percent" (the other rows follow the rule). (b) Report 1 omits the second footnote; the stored footnotes are `{noClose: 2, splitExcluded: 0}` and the built report prints zeros (read 12, "zeros print"; the screenshot of the evening briefing shows both), so the sample should end with "No rows were left out of the move counts and the named leaders by the split check." (c) Report 3's Summary says 2 of 12 "have fresh news"; one of the two is JONE, whose line (rightly) dates the news September 1, 34 days earlier, so the sample as printed contradicts itself; Marketing flagged the source defect, which is Research's; for his approval of the voice either use a checkpoint without such a row or say so in a line above the sample. (d) The header's "Covers $1 to $20" sits over two rows whose prior close is outside it: AVBP (28.46, noted) and MI in report 3 (0.894, not noted); both are in range now and the lines state the prior close or the move from it, so no wording changes, but add MI to the list of source rows. **Notes for Integration.** The deal-word fix ruled tonight (reads 75 to 75c, Research's `51da1b30`) makes the specimens' avoided words usable on a cited line: "agreed to sell", "entered an agreement", "Nasdaq confirmed that"; Marketing's item 3 (a voice that cannot say "sold", "buy" or "entered") is therefore no longer a limit once that ships, and the samples may be revised if he wants the plainer verbs. "The desk reads it as ..." sentences are rightly absent; the line "It is a proposal with a stated range, not a completed investment" (XHG) is the card's own restatement. **77. The TV desk reporter voice: the character paragraph and a check specimen (Marketing `61546d37`, `docs/marketing/voice-tv-desk-2026-10-12.md`; his direction in Marketing's chat, 2026-10-12: "a financial news tv desk reporter"), seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC): the figures and facts hold; four required changes to the wording; two to the character paragraph.** *Opened: the file; the INTO_POWER_HOUR raw card for ALEC (`eventAt` Oct 5, 7:00 AM ET, FRESH, age 8h), QTEX (the interconnect text, "measuring more than 100 dB channel isolation", the offering detail "approximately six weeks"), the checkpoint (RTH_INTERVAL completed 2026-10-05T18:30Z, 14:30 ET); the ruled frame and window words in `marketing.md`; the revision-line rule that a stored report read later must not show an unanchored time. Not run.* **Holds.** The specimen's figures are read 76's and are on the cards. ALEC: event Oct 5, 7:00 AM ET, the Genentech license of September 30, the runway into 2029, no terms, the May 2026 ATM up to $125.0 million. QTEX: "measured more than 100 dB channel isolation" is the card's own "measuring"; the offering of 11,111,111 shares at $0.90 on August 20 is six weeks (42 days) before the October 1 news, exact. MI's lines are the card's. "MI ranks first" is the narrative's "ranked first in this range". "Shares are up 20 percent from the prior close" is the same fact as "up 20 percent". **Required 1, relative day words.** "This morning Alector disclosed ..." and "MI leads the board this afternoon" are true at 14:30 ET on October 5 and untrue of any later reader of the stored report; the revision-line reads (31, 45) ruled that a stored report must not carry an unanchored time. Use the date: "On October 5 Alector disclosed ..." and, for the Summary, "at the 2:30 PM ET checkpoint" or no time (the checker admits a clock time equal to the checkpoint). "That morning" is acceptable only after the date has been stated in the same line. **Required 2, "the fresh headline".** "ALEC is the name with the fresh headline" and "has the fresh headline" say there is one; the report's own count is 2 (JONE is the other, 34 days old, read 76). Say "ALEC has fresh news" and "has fresh news in our sources", the narrative's words. **Required 3, "leads the board".** The narrative says "ranked first in this range", a rank by the desk's ranking, not by size of move; "leads the board" reads to a viewer as the biggest gainer, which is true of MI here and untrue of XHG in the after-hours report (up 25 percent while IPDN is up 50). Use "ranks first" in the Summary as in the line, never "leads". **Required 4, the promised new wording.** "Six weeks earlier" is exact but its antecedent is two sentences back; write "On August 20, six weeks before that, it completed ...". **The character paragraph adds no claim, with two additions.** "You are a financial news desk reporter on air" and "the viewer" are framing for the model, not statements on the page, provided the output never addresses the reader or refers to broadcast. **Add:** (a) never address the reader or a viewer ("you", "viewers", "stay with us", "back to you", "we are watching"): a TV voice drifts to it and it implies a broadcast and a relationship the product does not have; (b) never say what traders, investors or the market are doing, thinking or feeling ("traders are watching", "investors are pouncing", "all eyes on"): those are claims about third parties the desk does not hold, and a broadcast voice reaches for them first; the existing ban on words the facts do not define does not name them. Keep the rule that the desk reports what sources say and attributes it. His question, "is this the voice", is his. I did not run the checker.

**78. The voice, v4: name lines with no figure (Marketing `50622c04`, `docs/marketing/voice-tv-desk-2026-10-12.md` v4; Design's account of the card at `cdd8fe9d`), seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC): the hand-offs are true of the cards and the removals add no claim; two required changes.** *Opened: v4 whole (the paragraph, the specimen, the v3 and v4 additions), the INTO_POWER_HOUR cards for ALEC, MI and QTEX (price paths), the narrative's tense; no tense rule exists in `check.ts` or `writerV6.ts` (searched). Not run.* **The hand-offs are true.** ALEC: prior close 1.81, now 2.175 (up), high 3.51 (off the high, 38 percent under it). MI: 0.894 to 8.0996 (up), high 10.42 (off, 22 percent under). QTEX: 1.10 to 1.5813 (up), high 1.68 (off, 5.9 percent under). **The removals.** Dropping the percents, the ATM program (ALEC), the August offering (QTEX) and the June share count (MI) removes statements and adds none; the card prints the prices and the two computed figures above the line, so nothing is lost to a reader and no second, differently rounded number can print beside them (Design's reason, accepted). MI's placement stays as news and is the card's. **Required 1, the character paragraph contradicts v4.** The paragraph the prompt carries still says "the name first, then the move, then the news, then the one fact that comes next, such as share supply"; v4 says a name line states no move figure and no share-count sentence unless it is the news. A prompt that carries both will be pulled both ways, and the first is the one that produces the second, differently rounded number. Rewrite the sentence: the name first, a one-clause hand-off with no figure, then the news; no price or percent in a name line; a share-count or supply sentence only when it is the news. **Required 2, the hand-off rule and the tense.** (a) The writer must be told when each clause is true, from the card's own computed figures: "up" or "down from the prior close" by the sign of the card's figure, "little changed" when it rounds to 0.0; "off the high" only when the card prints a figure under the high of 1.0 percent or more, "at the high" only when it prints "at the high", and no clause about the high otherwise (a name 0.1 percent under its high is not "off the high"). (b) With the Summary's checkpoint time gone (v3: the checker strikes a 12-hour form) and the lines in the present tense ("Shares are up", "ranks first"), a stored report read later states a market condition as if it were now; the narrative's own convention is the past tense ("was up 806 percent", "ranked first in this range"). Say the checkpoint's state in the past tense, in the lines as in the Summary, or anchor it ("at the checkpoint"), as the paragraph already does for times. Not asked: whether the specimen is the voice (his). Design's statement that the card's "prior close" label is the close before the session the report is for, on both editions, matches the PREMARKET cards (basis TODAY_CLOSE, `from` the previous session's close, read 76). I did not run the checker.

**79. The voice, v6: structure loosened at his word (Marketing `0f3418cd`, `docs/marketing/voice-tv-desk-2026-10-12.md` v6; his words in Marketing's chat, "dont give it structure so rigidly. say what all must be included, but tell it to do it in a human style in the voice described. instruct it to not be robotic, but to be coherent"), seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC): the truth rules of reads 77 and 78 are all kept; loosening the structure opens four doors, each closed by one sentence; the specimen's facts hold.** *Opened: v6's paragraph and specimen, the three cards (ALEC, MI, QTEX) and the Summary against the narrative, `check.ts` rules named in v3 (own-ticker opening, `LEANS_ON_ANOTHER`, no clock time in a Summary), and reads 29, 36, 77 and 78. Not run.* **Kept, as Marketing says:** no cause, no forecast, no advice, no addressing the reader, no claim about traders or the market, no word the facts do not define, a rank said as a rank, past tense for the checkpoint, dates not clock times, own-ticker opening, "off the high" and "at the high" only as the card prints them. The "must include" list (the name; the news the desk found with its date as the sources state it; a supply fact only when it is the news; a few words on up or down from the prior close only as the card shows) adds no claim. The specimen's hand-offs are true of the cards as in read 78, and its Summary is the narrative's content in the past tense. **Required 1, "connected" and "one coherent piece of speech" collide with the own-ticker rule.** Each name's line is checked alone and may not name another ticker or open on "It", "Also", "The same", "Both" (check.ts); a writer told to connect the report will reach for "meanwhile", "by contrast", "the other name with fresh news", and be struck, or worse compare names in a way no figure supports. Say where coherence lives: within each line and in the Summary, which may relate names the narrative relates; a name line never refers to another name. **Required 2, connectives.** A natural, connected voice says "shares rose after the company said", "on the news", "amid", "following the filing", "driven by", "sparked": each one states or implies a cause for the move, which reads 29 and 36 ruled out and the paragraph's "never say what a headline caused" does not itself list. Add: state the move and the news in separate clauses; never join them with "after", "following", "on", "as", "amid", "thanks to", "driven by", "sparked", "triggered", "fueled". **Required 3, "in your own words".** For the direction clause, own words invite "jumped", "soared", "tumbled", "slumped", "spiked", "plunged", "rallied": magnitude and speed words with no figure in the line to define them. Allow only direction ("up", "down", "higher", "lower", "little changed"), plus the two ruled high clauses; "little changed" when the card's figure rounds to 0.0 (read 78). **Required 4, names with no news, and the stock phrases that must repeat.** "The news the desk found, with its date" and "do not repeat a stock phrase from name to name" would make a writer invent news for, or paraphrase, a name with none. The ruled state words must repeat exactly: "Not researched at this checkpoint", "no catalyst found in our sources", the "our research reached no conclusion" form; for those states say exactly that and nothing more, and add "except the ruled state phrases" to the no-repeat rule. **Advisory:** the example's "the company says the deal extends its cash runway into 2029" is present tense inside a paragraph that asks for the past tense; "said". The paragraph's remaining rules are unchanged from reads 77 and 78. Whether it is the voice is his. I did not run the checker.

**80. The three sample reports rewritten in the TV desk voice (Marketing `22325e02`, `docs/marketing/sample-reports-tv-2026-10-12.md`, 141 lines; his "rewrite all three reports in that voice"), seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC): the facts and every hand-off hold; two corrections.** *Opened: the file whole; the three raw JSONs (the same cards as read 76); by program, every name line's direction and "off its high" clause against the card's computed figures (36 lines; the card's own rules in `pathView`, `lib/dashboard/sessionReports.ts` 321 to 360: a change that rounds to zero is "flat", "X percent under the high" or "at the high", labels "AH high" and "AH close" for TODAY_CLOSE, "high" and "at {checkpoint time}" for PRIOR_CLOSE); by hand BRN, OLN and IPDN against their cards; the weekday claims against the calendar and the card event times; the connectives by reading. Not run: the checker or writer.* **Hand-offs (Marketing's question 1).** Every one is true of its card: direction by the card's sign (HSCS down, POLA, XCH, OLOX, CTVA, GLXG, QTEX up; MI, OLN, VRAX, NIQ flat at 0.0), "off its high" only where the card is 1.0 percent or more under (smallest case OLOX, 2.4 percent, then CTVA 2.2 and QTEX 5.9), and no high clause for OLN, VRAX, NIQ and JONE (0.3, 0.8, 0.5 and 0.1 percent under). Report 1's "after the close", "in the after-hours session" and "after-hours high" are the card's own labels ("AH high", "AH close"). The state phrases stand alone and are the ruled ones. Cut supply sentences remove claims and add none (Marketing's note lists each). BRN: the card says "a buyer option to acquire that royalty after closing for C$5 million"; "the buyer holds an option to purchase after closing for C$5 million" says the same (question 4). OLN: the approval was announced August 25 (event 4:45 PM ET), as the line says. IPDN "to finance a second" is the card's. Report 2's "came with no catalyst found in our sources" keeps the exact phrase, so the rule's lookahead passes it (question 5). **Required 1, "closed higher" and "closed lower" (question 2).** XHG, LCFY, KAPA and IPDN say "closed"; BRN, AIIO, VIVK and SXTP say "was up" or "lower". The card does not call its last price the close: for this report kind it prints "at {checkpoint time}" (`pathView`, PRIOR_CLOSE branch), a price at 16:00 that is the bell-time capture, and the stored bell capture is not the official close on most measured names (differences under 1 percent typical, up to 6; the first performance report found the same kind of gap). "Closed" claims a value the card does not carry. The direction is safe for these four (+25, -17, -27, +50 percent) but the word is not; use "was up from the prior close" and "was down from the prior close" in all eight lines, as the other four already do, and drop "for the day". **Required 2, weekday anchors.** "Friday evening our sources found a filing" (HSCS, event October 2, 5:15 PM ET, a Friday) and "Just after Monday's close" (POLA, October 5, 4:05 PM ET) are true, and break the paragraph's own rule that every time is anchored to a date: a stored report read in another week makes a weekday ambiguous. Write "On the evening of October 2" and "just after the October 5 close". **Advisories.** "Came with no catalyst found in our sources" reads as a statement about the arrival of the move; the narrative's "with no catalyst found in our sources" is the exact form. IPDN's "arranged a lease" replaces the card's "entered an equipment lease arrangement" to avoid a word the checker struck; once the deal-word fix ships (reads 75 to 75c) the source's verb "entered" is allowed on this cited line. Notes on the connectives: "followed by" (VRAX) and "after a one-for-12 reverse split" (SMXT) join two items of news in time, not a move and a news item, which is the rule; no line joins a move to news. The Summaries are the narratives' content in the past tense. Whether it is the voice is his.

**81. The v7 writer prompt, a truth read before the paid test (Research `32c00a76`, branch `research/writer-v7-on-2fa909bf`: `lib/desk/reports/writerV7.ts` `SYSTEM_V7`, `narrativeSheetV7`, `factSheetV7`, `cardMoveWords`; `docs/research/reports/writer-v7/plan.md`; Integration's ask), seventh tenure, 2026-10-12 (UTC): PASS with three required changes; answers to (a), (b), (c).** *Read: the file whole (232 lines, the system prompt in full), the plan, `pathView` for the card's rounding, the OLN and HSCS cards, reads 75 to 80. Not run; no model call.* **What holds.** The prompt carries read 79's four rules and reads 77 and 78's: coherence within a line and never a reference to another name; the move and its news in separate clauses with the word list; only up, down, higher, lower, little changed; the three ruled state phrases exactly; no clock time and every time anchored to a date; past tense with "still", "remains" and "continues" out; own-ticker opening with the checker's own list; rank said as a rank; nothing said about traders, investors or the market; the deal exception is read 75's shapes (agent, "agreed to sell", "an agreement to sell", "selling stockholders", "entered into an agreement"); "confirmed" is banned outright, stricter than my ruling and harmless ("the company reported"). `cardMoveWords` rounds as `pathView` does (one decimal; at the high when the figure rounds to 0.0 or below; off at 1.0 or more under), and the sheets give direction and high as words from the card's own figures. **Required 1, "on" and "as" in the join list.** The prompt says never to join a move and its news with "on" and "as" (among others) and quotes them. "On October 5, Alector disclosed ..." is the form the date rule requires; a model told never to use "on" will drop the preposition or the date. Add, in the same sentence: a date's "on" is fine; the words are forbidden only where they connect a move to news. **Required 2, the desk's gloss.** The name sheet gives "what the desk makes of it (restate, never add to it ...)" from `whyTradersMayCare`. That field is the desk's reading of significance and holds statements about what traders or the market may care about or think (OLN's card: "which may shape views of the combined company"; HSCS's: "possibly bearing on near-term liquidity or obligations"); restating it in a line is a claim about the market's views, which the prompt forbids two bullets later. Marketing's samples left every such sentence out for this reason. Drop the field from the sheet, and add to the prompt: restate what the source reports and its terms; never restate the desk's reading of what it may mean, or anything about what traders, investors or the market may think, do or care about. **Required 3, "covered" in the named movers (question b).** The recap sheet labels a named mover "(covered, the highest peak among the 50 percent movers)", and the narrative's own words are "the two covered 50 percent movers with the highest peaks"; the covered-count line is correctly out, but if the writer drops the word "covered" the Summary says two names have the highest peaks, which is untrue of the 50 percent movers the desk does not cover. Add to the must-include list that a named recap mover is called a covered 50 percent mover with a high peak, as the narrative says. **(a) A name with no card path keeps its price and framed change: yes**, only when the sheet says no card path, with the change's frame in the clause and the past tense; the voice's "never a price" rests on the card printing one, and a line with neither leaves the reader nothing. **(c) Under the high by less than 1.0 percent, say nothing about the high: yes**, it is the right reading of "only as the card shows" (the card prints "0.3% under the high" there, not "at the high" and not a phrase of ours). **(b) Keep the covered-movers count out:** yes, with Required 3. Advisory: the kind frames ("Into the open: the session so far") are v6's and carry no time; fine. The `NEWS_STATE` text for CONFLICTING gives no ruled phrase and leaves the model to say the sources conflict, which is the ruled heading's meaning. Whether the voice is right is his, from the pairs.

**82. His rule "i recommend we dont strike words. the model should be smart enough to follow a rule given appropriate instruction." (Research chat 39e1d5a3, 2026-10-13T01:29:23.576Z, via Integration; Integration's scope: word-list strikes go and their rules move into the writer prompt; fact checks stay; the em dash and the advice line go to him), seventh tenure, 2026-10-13 (UTC): what a prompt can carry and what it cannot.** *Opened for this answer: `docs/research/session-reports/commercial.md` sections 1 to 3 (the publisher's exclusion, 15 U.S.C. 80b-2(a)(11)(D), and the "impersonal" condition; the standing "We are not an investment adviser" disclaimer), `monetization-gates.md` on redistribution, and my own reads 1 to 81 by recall of what each ruled; nothing run.* **(1) Rulings that are a compliance requirement a prompt alone cannot carry, by name.** (a) **Not advice.** The product's posture is a publisher of impersonal information, and its terms say nothing it displays is "a suggestion to buy or sell any security"; a report line that tells or suggests a reader to buy, sell, enter, exit, add, hold, avoid, short, fade, chase or wait is advice in the report's own voice. This is the one ruling in reads 1 to 81 whose failure is a legal-posture failure rather than an accuracy one, and a writer that is told not to is a control with no second key; it needs an independent check (below). (b) **No forecast and no promise of an outcome** (the charter's prohibition 2, the claims rules, reads 6, 29, 36, 77): "will", "set to", "poised", "headed" about a price. Same class and same check. (c) **Statements of fact that must match the data**, which Integration's scope already keeps as fact checks: numbers, dates, clock times, ranks, tickers, a share count with its date and never called float, a percent with its frame (the "value with no frame" defect), a halt only from the stored field; these are accuracy and misleading-statement controls and must stay deterministic because a model is worst at checking its own arithmetic. (d) **What the page may not say about its own coverage:** "no catalyst" as a statement about the market, "the real catalyst", "every name", a cause for a move (reads 29, 36): accuracy rather than law; the state phrases come from the stored status, and the model checker's UNSUPPORTED read holds UNKNOWN to "reached no conclusion"; a prompt plus that read is enough if the read is kept. Nothing else I ruled is a compliance requirement: the connectives, the move words, the tense, the own-ticker opening, the clock times, "confirmed", "agreed to sell" (my reads 75 to 75c) and the rest are voice or accuracy rules; if the prompt carries them and the checks above stay, losing a regex costs a line, not a posture. The own-ticker rule is a display requirement (a line must stand alone on its card), so it can move to the model checker's self-containedness read, not to the prompt alone. **Insider names and holdings** never print on reports because the sheets do not contain them (read 23b): a data control, not an instruction, and it must stay that way. **"Restate, never quote"** protects against reproducing vendor text; his 2026-10-09 acceptance (reads 34a, 34b) covers headlines, so it is a courtesy and a prompt instruction is enough. **The em dash** is his rule; a mechanical replacement is compliance with it, but a blind replacement can move a clause boundary (the privacy-policy precedent): replace by punctuation that keeps the clause, or let the writer retry, and measure the lines that change. **(2) The minimum check for advice if not a word list.** (i) **An independent model read on every line and the Summary**, by the checker model, with two yes-or-no questions and the quoted phrase when yes: *does this text tell, suggest or imply that a reader should or could buy, sell, enter, exit, add to, hold, avoid, short, fade, chase, wait for or watch a security, or recommend any action on it*, and *does it predict or promise what a price will do or any outcome*. A yes strikes the line. The rubric carries the carve-out in words and with six positive examples drawn from the 46 real deal lines ("agreed to sell", "entered an agreement to sell", "Nasdaq confirmed it had regained compliance", "direct Hudson Global Ventures to buy up to $125 million"): a company's or counterparty's own action stated as a fact is not advice. (ii) **A pre-registered gate before the word list is switched off**: a seeded set of at least 40 advice and forecast sentences, explicit ("sell into strength", "worth buying on the dip", "entry at") and soft ("worth a look", "one to watch", "time to take profits", "a name to keep an eye on", "dip buyers", "good entry", "stand aside", "cheap", "poised to"), plus compounds ("agreed to sell ... so sell into strength"); the model read must strike every one in three runs of the set (it is not deterministic), and must pass at least 95 percent of the 46 real deal lines. A single miss on the seeded set keeps a narrow deterministic backstop of the unambiguous imperative shapes only, as a flag and a strike, until the model read is changed and re-run. (iii) **Audit after switch-off**: the struck text is now kept (Research's change); a nightly scan of every stored report by a different model for the same two questions, with every hit sent to Integration the next morning, for the first thirty days, and a switch that restores the word list in one change. (iv) The standing disclaimer at the entry gate stays as the third layer; it does not replace the first two. I accept his rule for every rule in (1)(d) and the voice rules, and I do not accept it for (a) and (b) without (i) to (iii); that is for him, through Integration, in one sentence: the model may be trusted to follow the rule, and the second key is an independent reader of the same line. The deal-word shapes of reads 75 to 75c become moot if the word list goes; their 46 real lines and 22 negatives are the positive and negative examples for the rubric and the seed set.

**82a. A correction to read 82 (a), made before it was sent, seventh tenure, 2026-10-13 (UTC).** Read 82 called an advice line in a report "a legal-posture failure". The law I quoted in `commercial.md` section 1 does not say that: under 15 U.S.C. 80b-2(a)(11)(D) and Lowe v. SEC (472 U.S. 181) a bona fide publication of general and regular circulation is outside the Act even when it contains advice and comment about specific securities, because it is impersonal; advice in a report would not by itself make the publisher an adviser. What the record supports is narrower: "no buy/sell language, ever" is the product's own rule (`assessment.ts`) and the standing disclaimer's posture; whether an invite-only desk is a publication of "general and regular circulation", and what state law adds, is the open question section 7 left to him ("whether this needs a lawyer"). So the advice check is a control on an unresolved legal risk and on his own product rule, not a proven legal requirement; I would not treat the absence of an independent check as acceptable until a lawyer says the exclusion holds without it, and a free-text writer is a new way for individualised-sounding or directive language to enter. The recommendation in read 82 (2) stands on that footing.

**85. The independent second read (Research `3c1c112a`, `lib/desk/reports/check.ts`, `SECOND_READ_V6`, `ADVICE_QUESTION`, `FORECAST_QUESTION`, `DEAL_EXAMPLES`), seventh tenure, 2026-10-13 (UTC).** Opened the file at that commit. My two questions are verbatim in substance; a yes strikes with the quoted phrase; a missing answer fails closed. Four answers to the words Research added:

1. **Soft forms in the advice question ("worth a look", "one to watch", "a name to keep an eye on", "good entry", "stand aside"): keep them in the prompt, and keep the gate clean another way.** They help the read in production. The gate counts only seed sentences that do not use those five phrasings (the 40 or more must be new soft forms, explicit forms and compounds); the five go in a separate in-sample line of the report, as the 8 of 46 deal lines are.
2. **Attributed company statements (the ALEC "the company said the deal extends its cash runway into 2029"): REQUIRED, add to the carve-out.** A struck attributed forecast is not the intended result. The forecast question is about the desk's own voice. The approved samples already depend on it: ALEC's runway into 2029; AIIO "said it expected about US$600 million of revenue for 2026"; SXTP results "in January 2027"; LCFY "expects about $2.0 million in ARR"; AIFA "a formal launch was expected"; OLN "Wells Fargo kept its Equal-Weight rating and lowered its target to $16". Words for the carve-out: "a statement, plan, expectation, guidance or target attributed to a named company or source and reported as theirs (they said, expect, plan, set a target) is a fact about what they said, not a prediction by us. Not covered: the line adopting it in its own voice ('will', 'is set to', 'poised to'), a price prediction not attributed, or any of it aimed at a reader." Put these six lines into the must-pass set with the 46.
3. **Seed boundary cases for the gate, with the answer I rule now.** (a) "The company said its shares will double" and "management expects the stock to re-rate": the attribution does not launder a price promise about the stock; STRIKE (the carve-out covers a company's statements about its business and events and an attributed analyst target, not a promise about its own share price). (b) An attributed rating change ("Wells Fargo raised its rating to Buy"): this is the one case I do not rule alone. The product's rule is "no buy/sell language"; an attributed analyst action is reporting, which the exclusion tolerates, but the word list used to strike it. Put it in the seed set as a labelled boundary line, report it apart from the gate, and I will put the call to Integration for Adam if the read passes it.
4. **The self-containedness question: one wording fix, REQUIRED.** "or speaks of another name" is ambiguous: a name line says "Genentech", "NFT Ltd", "Solidion", all of which are names. The rule I ruled and the checker enforces is another covered TICKER. Say "or names another ticker on the board". The opening list as examples is fine. Note: the carve-out's first example uses "confirmed", a word the writer prompt bans; harmless in the checker (it never produces it), but a reader of the prompt may take it as licence; say "an example of a counterparty's own action" and keep it.

**89. Float fix 1, the filed count in place of "Cannot verify" (Design's sketch, message of 2026-10-13; his ruling relayed by Integration 39th: "3, yes, when can't verify place a very small icon or somethign with a tooltip and link toexplanation"), seventh tenure.** Read `lib/dashboard/floatBound.ts` at `2fa909bf` (the six refused reasons and their stored sentences, the legend, the click-through words, `cannotVerifyText`), its consumers (`FloatCell.tsx`, `deskRead.ts`, `drawerFloat.ts`), and the landing line at `lib/public/landingCopy.ts:139`.

- **Which refusals may show a number.** The reasons are not alike. For COUNT_TOO_OLD, ISSUANCE_FLAGGED and ISSUANCE_FILED, ISSUANCE_NOT_CHECKED, FILINGS_NOT_REREAD and UNREGISTERED_SALE_NOT_CHECKABLE the filed count is a parsed filed number and the doubt is its age or the supply since: it may print, marked. For SPLIT_SINCE_COUNT (the count "may be in different share units", so the number could be wrong by the split factor) it must not print; Design already recommends that and I agree. For BOUND_OUT_OF_RANGE ("the computed figure did not pass the desk's own sanity check") it must not print until Research says what that check reads: I have not opened it, and if the count is what failed, the face would print the number the check rejected. The no-reason fault case keeps "Cannot verify" with no number.
- **Design's question (a): "filed 12.4M 9/22" under the header "TMI Float" is not enough.** "Filed" says where the number comes from and not what it is; under a float header a bare figure reads as a float figure, and for the issuance reasons it can be well below the true share count (the very case the bound refused for). The face must carry the unit: a word that says the number is a count of shares (Marketing's to word, for example "12.4M sh" or "12.4M o/s"). If width allows only one of "filed" and the unit word, the unit word stays on the face and "filed" and the date go to the hover; the icon is always on. With the unit on the face, the header's hover gains the one sentence that makes the header honest: where a TMI Float cannot be verified, the cell shows the company's filed share count, which is shares outstanding with no insider holdings subtracted, and it is not a TMI Float. With both, A1 satisfies (a) and I do not require A2 or A3. If the measure forces a bare "12.4M" and an icon, it fails (a) and the choice between a header change and a hover-only count is his, through Integration.
- **Three existing statements the change contradicts and must carry in the same release.** (i) The public page's own definition, "Cannot verify is a count that exists but cannot be relied on today" (`docs/marketing/float-page-draft.md`): the face now prints that count, so the page says it is shown as filed and not relied on. (ii) The landing sentence (`landingCopy.ts:139`): it lists the faces as the ceiling, Unknown, Cannot verify or Not computed; it needs the filed-count face, and the canvas copies (`Main.dc.html`, `LandingMobile.dc.html`) are independent and need the same edit. (iii) The click-through panel's headline ("TMI Float ... no more than {x} shares") must not be used for this face; its headline is that the desk could not verify a TMI Float and the latest filed share count is {shares} as of {date}, from a {form} filed {date}.
- **Surfaces.** Sort, any filter, any comparison and any export use bounds only; the filed count never enters them (Design says the sort already does; a test should pin the filter and the Desk Read too). The Desk Read prints the same face with the same unit word. No "≤" and no bound-kind on this face (pinned). Ink-2 against ink is a visual cue and not a label: the unit word and the icon carry the meaning, and the accessible name says it in words ("filed share count {x} as of {date}; TMI Float could not be verified: {stored reason}").
- **Agreed as Design has it:** the drawer shows the reason and never "at most" over a filed count (b); stored reasons only (c); no filed count keeps the word "Cannot verify", never blank (d). Marketing words, Design measures at 1,024 px, built strings come back to me; it goes to him before ship.

**89a. The face "filed 12.4M" with the date in the hover (Design, 2026-10-13: measured 110.9 px for "filed 12.4M 9/22" plus icon against 86 px of content; "filed 12.4M" plus icon is 83.3 px; Integration ruled A1 and the header stays "TMI Float"), seventh tenure.** Messages crossed with read 89, which already ruled the fallback: the unit word stays on the face, and "filed" and the date may go to the hover. **"filed 12.4M" does not meet it, REQUIRED change:** "filed" says where the figure is from and not what it is, and under a float header it reads as a filed float. Use the unit word instead of "filed" ("12.4M sh" or "12.4M o/s", Marketing's to word; "filed" moves to the hover and the panel). By Design's own method " sh" is narrower than "filed " (about 16 px against about 20), so it fits inside the 86 px with the icon. **The date off the face is acceptable only with a rule for the age reason:** the bound's face carries the date because the claim is dated; a count the desk calls too old to rely on is a stale number by definition, and printed with no date it reads as current. So COUNT_TOO_OLD keeps the word "Cannot verify" with no number on the face and the count and its date in the hover and the panel; the other printed reasons stand (the icon is always on, its hover gives the stored reason and the count's date, and the header sentence says what the number is). This amends read 89's first point for COUNT_TOO_OLD only.

**89b. The order of the refusal checks, and what may print (Design's finding, opened by me in `lib/research/insiderBound.ts` at `2fa909bf`), seventh tenure, 2026-10-13.** The function returns at the first failing check, in this order: ISSUANCE_FILED (line 323), the 20-F/40-F case (326), ISSUANCE_NOT_CHECKED (328), ISSUANCE_FLAGGED (329), SPLIT_SINCE_COUNT (330), a count dated after the checkpoint as BOUND_OUT_OF_RANGE (333), COUNT_TOO_OLD (334), and BOUND_OUT_OF_RANGE again for a bound that is not positive or exceeds the count (345). Design's reading is right and read 89's first point needs two corrections. (1) A row refused at 323 to 329 was never tested for a split, so its filed count can be in pre-split units: it prints no number until Research carries the split result on the payload. (2) The same rows were never tested for age either, so their count may be older than the 90-day maximum: age can be tested at display from `countAsOf` and the board's date with his 90-day rule, or Research carries it too. **Rule: a refused face prints the filed count only where both the split check and the age check were run and passed.** On the present order that is a row refused at 345 (out of range, which read 89 already holds out) or by a view-layer reason such as FILINGS_NOT_REREAD, so almost nothing prints until Research's payload field exists. That is the correct size for the change today; the cell keeps the word "Cannot verify" everywhere else.

**89c. Faces that stay the word, and when the new copy may ship (Design, measured: "12.4M sh" plus icon 63.9 px, "12.4M o/s" plus icon 70.4 px, both fit 86; "12.4M sh 9/22" plus icon 91.4 px does not; "Cannot verify" plus icon 96.3 px does not fit), seventh tenure, 2026-10-13.** (1) **The word alone, no icon, on the faces that print no number** (COUNT_TOO_OLD, SPLIT_SINCE_COUNT, BOUND_OUT_OF_RANGE, the fault case, and any row without the split field): the word is the state, and a bare icon in its place makes a reader hover to learn what the cell says. Every face already opens the panel, so the reason and the link are one click away as today. Putting an icon on those faces too would be a column-width decision, his, and I do not recommend it. (2) **With Integration's rule (the count prints only when Research's split field is NONE, never for BOUND_OUT_OF_RANGE, and a missing field means no number) and the age rule of 89b, no row prints a number today. The new public and header copy must therefore not ship before the code that prints the number.** The header sentence ("where a TMI Float cannot be verified, the cell shows the company's filed share count ... not a TMI Float"), the page's section, the landing sentence and its canvases describe a state no cell would be in; the sentence would be false on the day it ships. They ship in the same release as Research's field and the face, and not before. (3) **A test that the gate admits:** a fixture row with a filed count, split NONE, age within 90 days and a reason that passes the other rules must print the number; a gate that refuses everyone looks the same as a correctly empty one. Verify on production, once the field is live, that at least one real row prints, before the header sentence is called true.

**89d. A correction to 89b (Design's, confirmed by me in `lib/research/floatRead.ts` at `2fa909bf`), seventh tenure, 2026-10-13.** 89b said a row refused for FILINGS_NOT_REREAD, "a view-layer reason", was the one case that could print. I had not opened where that reason is raised. `floatCellAt` returns it at line 27, before `floatCellFor` (and so before the split and age checks) runs, so no refused row prints a number today; the whole feature waits on Research's split field. The error is mine: a case named from a guess about where a reason is raised, in a read whose point was the order of the checks. Age at display needs the board's date, and reports and exports have none (`FloatSurface.dateShown` absent), so on those surfaces a refused row prints the word only unless Research carries the age result too; either meets the rule, and Research carrying it is the simpler.

**90. The rating-change carve-out (Research `f128ea80`; checker `ANALYST_RATING_CHANGE` in `lib/desk/reports/check.ts`, writer v6 prompt version 16 in `writerV6.ts`; his yes "2, yes" relayed by Integration 39th, 2026-10-13T09:39:59.808Z), seventh tenure.** Opened the diff, the file at that commit, and ran the pattern in node against 22 lines (the buy/sell test only; the other FORBIDDEN rules still test the remainder, so "..., a good entry" strikes on enter/entry as before).

- **The checker half works as claimed.** Passes: "Wells Fargo raised its rating to Buy.", "Jefferies initiated coverage of the company with a Buy rating.", "Piper Sandler upgraded the stock to Buy.", "Roth Capital reiterated its Buy rating on October 6.", "H.C. Wainwright downgraded the shares to Sell.", "B. Riley Securities maintained a Buy rating on the company.", and "Wells Fargo kept its Equal-Weight rating and lowered its target to $16." Struck: "Analysts say to buy the dip.", "Analysts direct investors to buy shares.", "Wells Fargo raised its rating to Buy, so buy the stock.", "... and told clients to buy more.", "Analysts raised their rating to Buy.", "Several firms upgraded the stock to Buy.", "It raised its rating to Buy.", "The stock upgraded to Buy.", a lower-case "to buy", "Wells Fargo analysts raised the rating to Buy." (conservative: the party must be capitalised words only), and "Investors upgraded the stock to Buy." It applies only on a name line with cited research (`dealFactsAllowed`); the Summary (no ticker) keeps every buy/sell word struck, so a rating can be stated in a name's line and not in the Summary, which matches the writer sentence ("in that name's line").
- **Advisory, cheap to fix:** the unnamed-crowd exclusion applies only at the start of the match, so the engine can start the match one word later: "Traders Union upgraded the stock to Buy." removes "Union upgraded the stock to Buy" and leaves "Traders" behind, and the line passes. It needs a crowd word immediately before a capitalised word, so it is contrived; add a look-behind that the party is not preceded by a capitalised word (so the engine cannot slide past an excluded one) and a test.
- **REQUIRED, the writer half contradicts itself.** The v16 sentence tells the writer to state a named firm's rating capitalised ("Wells Fargo raised its rating to Buy"), and the line immediately before it in the same prompt ("An analyst rating of Buy or Sell is 'a positive (or negative) analyst rating'", under the USING THE FACTS rules) still says the opposite for every rating. Reconcile: keep that earlier rule for a rating with no named firm (the checker still strikes it) and say so, e.g. "An analyst rating of Buy or Sell with no firm named is 'a positive (or negative) analyst rating'." Otherwise the writer follows either one.
- **Integration's two questions.** (1) "reiterated", "maintained", "kept": reporting, not a repeated recommendation, because the line says what a named firm did, in its own name, on a date; the OLN sample ("kept its Equal-Weight rating") already passed in read 80. One point for Adam, in a clause and not a question: his words were "rating changes"; the carve-out also admits a firm repeating or keeping its rating. I would not hold it for that. (2) "Target": no form is needed. "Lowered its target to $16" carries no buy/sell word, so the rule never tests it, and the independent read (88) already treats an attributed target as reporting. A target with a forecast word ("will reach") still strikes on "will".
- **Note for the writer v7 prompt (version 4), when the word strikes go:** it has no rating sentence. The independent read passed the rating line 3 of 3 and struck the instruction forms 3 of 3, so nothing is unsafe, but the v7 writer is neither told it may state one nor that it must not; add the same sentence there when v7 is wired.

**89e. His ruling supersedes 89a (COUNT_TOO_OLD prints no number) and 89b (print only where the split and age checks passed), seventh tenure, 2026-10-13 (UTC).** Integration 39th quotes him (Integration chat 1f30f0a2, 2026-10-13T09:57:22.409Z): "3, no show a number and another icon with a link and a tooltip, yes, with warning.", answering "Show no number when there's been a share split, or the desk hasn't checked for one?" (no) and "Show a filed count older than 90 days, with the warning icon and its age in the hover?" (yes, with warning). My view was put to him in the block. It is his product; I take the ruling. The build: every refused row with a filed count prints it, with a distinct WARNING icon where a split was found, unproven or not run, or the count is older than 90 days, and the caveat icon otherwise; still no number for a count dated after the board date or a bound at or below zero. **My read of the words becomes: they must be TRUE, and the claim risk moves from the face to the words around it.** Conditions for the strings: (1) the warning hover says plainly, by case: a split found or unproven ("this count may be in pre-split share units"), no split check run ("the desk has not checked for a share split since this count"), a count over 90 days ("this count is from {date}, more than {n} days ago"); the case is never softened to "may be out of date". (2) The caveat hover for the issuance reasons says shares may have been issued since, so the true count may be HIGHER; the stored reason sentence does that today. (3) The header sentence and the page must not say or imply the number is current, latest, or a float: it is the company's filed share count at its date, with no insider holdings subtracted, shown where a TMI Float could not be verified, and not a TMI Float. (4) The two icons differ by SHAPE as well as colour, and the accessible name says the warning in words ("filed share count {x} as of {date}; may be in pre-split units"), never only "caveat" or "warning". (5) The unit word stays on the face ("sh" or "o/s"); any report, export or other surface that prints the cell prints the face string and the warning in words, never the bare number; sort, filter and comparison stay bounds-only. (6) The copy still ships with the code that prints the number, and a test that the gate admits a qualifying row stays; with this ruling the number prints today, so the live check is a production row showing the warning icon, not a wait for Research's field.
